# Seri 19 - Workflow / Camunda / Saga Support Pattern

Status: **Seri 19 dari 22 seri lanjutan**.  
Setelah seri ini selesai, tersisa **3 seri lagi**.

---

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas bagaimana Redis bisa membantu workflow berbasis:

```text
Java 17+
Jersey/JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Camunda 7 / Camunda 8
Redis
Kafka
Docker / Kubernetes
```

Fokusnya:

```text
- workflow correlation cache
- process instance lookup helper
- external task / job worker dedup
- Camunda message correlation helper
- saga timeout helper
- transient workflow state
- workflow progress cache
- worker throttling
- command idempotency
- boundary tegas antara Redis, Camunda, PostgreSQL, dan Kafka
```

Camunda 7 external task pattern bekerja dengan engine membuat external task, worker melakukan **fetch and lock**, lalu worker menyelesaikan task; lock berbasis timestamp mencegah task yang sama diambil beberapa worker selama lock masih valid, tetapi task bisa diambil lagi setelah lock expired. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/user-guide/process-engine/external-tasks/)) Camunda 8 job worker juga bersifat worker-driven: job diaktifkan dengan timeout, job bisa direassign jika timeout, dan dokumentasinya menekankan bahwa worker code harus idempotent karena job delivery bersifat at-least-once. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/job-workers/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text
Camunda
= workflow/process lifecycle owner
= BPMN state
= job/external task lifecycle
= timer/event/process history

PostgreSQL
= business source of truth
= case/order/payment/application state
= idempotency record
= audit/outbox/job state

Kafka
= durable cross-service domain/integration event backbone

Redis
= helper/control-plane/cache
= temporary correlation lookup
= idempotency fast gate
= worker throttle/semaphore
= transient progress/status cache
= local notification bridge
= timeout/wakeup helper, not final timer truth
```

Kalimat kunci:

> Redis boleh membantu workflow agar lebih cepat dan resilient, tetapi Redis tidak boleh menjadi workflow engine kedua.

---

## 3. Diagram Besar

```text
HTTP Command
  ↓
JAX-RS Resource
  ↓
Application Command Service
  ↓
Idempotency Gate
  ├── Redis fast idempotency state
  └── PostgreSQL final idempotency record / unique constraint
  ↓
PostgreSQL Transaction
  ├── update business state
  ├── insert outbox event
  └── insert workflow operation record
  ↓
Camunda Gateway / Client
  ├── start process
  ├── correlate message
  ├── complete external task / job
  └── fail/retry job
  ↓
Redis Helper Layer
  ├── correlation cache
  ├── process lookup cache
  ├── worker semaphore
  ├── dedup key
  ├── progress cache
  └── websocket notification signal
```

Boundary:

```text
Camunda decides process state.
PostgreSQL decides business state.
Redis accelerates lookup and coordination.
Kafka carries durable domain events.
```

---

## 4. Pattern Map

| Pattern | Redis role | Source of truth |
|---|---|---|
| Process instance lookup cache | Cache `businessKey -> processInstanceId/key` | Camunda/PostgreSQL |
| Message correlation helper | Cache correlation metadata | PostgreSQL/Camunda |
| External task/job dedup | Fast dedup key | PostgreSQL idempotency/job table |
| Worker concurrency throttle | Semaphore/rate limiter | Operational policy |
| Workflow progress cache | Fast read projection | Camunda/PostgreSQL |
| Saga participant temporary state | Short-lived helper | Saga state table / Camunda |
| Timeout wake-up helper | Signal only | Camunda timer / DB scheduler |
| Local notification bridge | Pub/Sub/WebSocket signal | Durable state elsewhere |
| Compensation hint cache | Optimization only | Saga/workflow history |
| Process start idempotency | Redis fast gate | PostgreSQL unique constraint |

---

## 5. Camunda 7 vs Camunda 8 Boundary

### Camunda 7 External Task

Camunda 7 external tasks are created by the process engine and fetched/locked by external workers. The lock prevents another worker from fetching the same task until the lock expires; when the external worker completes the work, it signals the engine to continue process execution. ([Camunda Documentation](https://docs.camunda.org/manual/latest/user-guide/process-engine/external-tasks/))

Redis can help with:

```text
- worker dedup
- external side effect idempotency
- worker concurrency cap
- external task result cache
- lock extension monitoring helper
```

Redis must not replace:

```text
- Camunda external task lock
- Camunda process state
- Camunda incident/retry semantics
```

### Camunda 8 Job Worker

Camunda 8 job workers activate jobs by type, complete or fail them, and if a job is not completed or failed within its activation timeout, it may be reassigned to another worker. The docs explicitly warn that this can lead to two workers working on the same job, and therefore worker code must be idempotent. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/job-workers/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Redis can help with:

```text
- idempotent worker guard
- dedup by job key / business key
- downstream concurrency semaphore
- worker-local progress cache
```

Redis must not be the final guard. The final guard should be:

```text
- PostgreSQL job/outbound request state
- idempotency table
- unique constraints
- external provider idempotency key
- Camunda job completion/failure result
```

---

## 6. Camunda Message Correlation Helper

Camunda 8 message correlation uses a message name and correlation key; if a published message matches an open subscription, it is correlated to the corresponding process instance. If no proper subscription is open, the message is discarded. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/messages/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Redis can cache correlation metadata:

```text
workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:corr:v1:order:o123
```

Value:

```json
{
  "processType": "order-fulfillment",
  "businessKey": "order:o123",
  "correlationKey": "order:o123",
  "processInstanceKey": "2251799813685251",
  "lastKnownState": "WAITING_PAYMENT",
  "cachedAt": "2026-07-13T18:00:00+07:00"
}
```

But the safe rule is:

```text
Redis correlation cache may help find where to send/check.
PostgreSQL/Camunda must decide whether correlation is valid.
```

Do not do this:

```text
Redis says process is WAITING_PAYMENT
  ↓
blindly publish payment message
```

Do this:

```text
Redis gives correlation hint
  ↓
check business state / operation record
  ↓
publish/correlate message idempotently
  ↓
handle no subscription / duplicate / stale state
```

---

## 7. Process Instance Lookup Cache

Use case:

```text
GET /cases/{caseId}/workflow
```

You may need to show:

```text
- process instance id/key
- current high-level workflow state
- active task summary
- last updated timestamp
```

Redis key:

```text
case-svc:prod:tenant:t1:workflow-cache:v1:case:c991
```

Value:

```json
{
  "caseId": "c991",
  "processInstanceId": "camunda7-id-or-c8-key",
  "processDefinitionKey": "case-review-v3",
  "workflowState": "WAITING_REVIEW",
  "activeTaskNames": ["Review Case"],
  "sourceVersion": 42,
  "cachedAt": "2026-07-13T18:10:00+07:00"
}
```

TTL:

```text
30 seconds - 5 minutes
```

Cocok untuk UI/read projection. Tidak cocok untuk deciding final workflow transition.

---

## 8. Process Start Idempotency

Problem:

```text
Client retries POST /cases/{caseId}/submit
  ↓
process started twice
```

Safe pattern:

```text
Idempotency-Key required
  ↓
Redis checkOrStart
  ↓
PostgreSQL transaction:
    update case status
    insert workflow_operation record
    insert outbox CaseSubmitted
  ↓
start/correlate Camunda process
  ↓
mark operation completed
```

PostgreSQL guard:

```sql
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_workflow_operation
ON workflow_operations (
  tenant_id,
  operation_type,
  business_key,
  idempotency_key_hash
);
```

Alternative guard:

```sql
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_active_case_process
ON case_workflows (
  tenant_id,
  case_id,
  process_type
)
WHERE status IN ('STARTING', 'RUNNING');
```

Redis fast gate improves latency. PostgreSQL unique constraint prevents duplicate process start if Redis fails.

---

## 9. Workflow Operation Table

For important workflow commands, use a durable operation table.

```sql
CREATE TABLE workflow_operations (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    operation_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    business_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    idempotency_key_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
    request_hash TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    camunda_process_instance_id TEXT NULL,
    camunda_job_key TEXT NULL,
    result JSONB NULL,
    error_code TEXT NULL,
    error_message TEXT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (tenant_id, operation_type, idempotency_key_hash)
);
```

Status:

```text
PROCESSING
CAMUNDA_REQUESTED
COMPLETED
FAILED_RETRYABLE
FAILED_FINAL
UNKNOWN_NEEDS_RECONCILIATION
```

This table gives you a recovery path when:

```text
- HTTP request times out
- Camunda command succeeds but response is lost
- Redis idempotency state expires
- worker crashes before marking completed
- duplicate command arrives
```

---

## 10. External Task / Job Worker Dedup

Camunda workers must be idempotent because tasks/jobs can be delivered more than once after timeout or retry. Camunda 8 docs explicitly state job worker code must tolerate execution more than once for the same job while preserving application state. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/job-workers/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Redis fast dedup key:

```text
workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:dedup:camunda-job:v1:job:{jobKeyHash}
```

Command:

```text
SET dedupKey PROCESSING NX EX 3600
```

But final guard should be in PostgreSQL:

```sql
CREATE TABLE processed_workflow_jobs (
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    job_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    worker_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    business_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    processed_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    result_ref TEXT NULL,

    PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, job_key, worker_type)
);
```

Worker flow:

```text
Receive Camunda job/external task
  ↓
Redis SET NX EX fast dedup
  ↓
PostgreSQL insert processed_workflow_jobs
  ├── success -> process
  └── conflict -> already processed, complete/ignore safely depending Camunda state
  ↓
perform side effect idempotently
  ↓
complete/fail Camunda job
```

---

## 11. External Side Effect in Workflow

Example:

```text
Camunda job: submit document to external regulator
```

Bad:

```text
Worker receives job
  ↓
call external API with random request ID
  ↓
complete Camunda job
```

If worker times out/retries, external regulator can receive duplicates.

Better:

```text
Worker receives job
  ↓
create/load outbound_request in PostgreSQL
  ↓
providerRequestId = deterministic id
  ↓
call external API with providerRequestId
  ↓
store provider response
  ↓
complete Camunda job
```

Redis can help with:

```text
- semaphore: max concurrent calls
- fast dedup
- temporary response cache
- retry throttle
```

But correctness must come from:

```text
- outbound_request table
- provider idempotency key
- job operation state
- Camunda job completion semantics
```

---

## 12. Worker Concurrency Throttling

Use case:

```text
Camunda creates many jobs of type SEND_NOTICE.
External notice provider allows only 10 concurrent requests.
```

Redis semaphore:

```text
workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:semaphore:notice-provider:v1
```

Flow:

```text
Worker activates job
  ↓
try acquire Redis semaphore permit
  ├── denied -> fail job with retry/backoff or delay locally
  └── acquired -> call provider
        ↓
      release permit
```

For Camunda 8, fail job commands can include remaining retries and retry backoff, allowing retry delay when external systems are down or overloaded. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/job-workers/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 13. Saga: Orchestrated vs Choreographed

### Orchestrated Saga

```text
Camunda owns saga flow
  ↓
Service tasks call participants
  ↓
Compensation modeled in BPMN
```

Use Camunda for:

```text
- long-running process
- compensation
- human tasks
- timeouts
- escalation
- state visibility
```

Redis role:

```text
- transient correlation cache
- participant call dedup
- worker throttling
- progress cache
- wake-up signal
```

### Choreographed Saga

```text
Services publish/consume events
  ↓
Kafka carries durable events
  ↓
Each service updates local state
```

Redis role:

```text
- local cache
- dedup helper
- rate limiter
- temporary state
```

Do not use Redis Pub/Sub as the durable saga event backbone.

---

## 14. Saga State Boundary

Bad:

```text
Redis hash = saga state
  status PAYMENT_DONE
  status INVENTORY_RESERVED
  status SHIPPED
```

If Redis is lost/evicted, saga state is lost.

Better:

```text
Camunda process instance or PostgreSQL saga table = source of truth
Redis = fast projection/cache
```

PostgreSQL saga table example:

```sql
CREATE TABLE saga_instances (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    saga_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    business_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    current_step TEXT NOT NULL,
    version BIGINT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    UNIQUE (tenant_id, saga_type, business_key)
);
```

Redis projection key:

```text
order-svc:prod:tenant:t1:saga-cache:v1:order:o123
```

---

## 15. Timeout Helper Pattern

Redis TTL/keyspace notification can be used as a **wake-up signal**, not final timer truth.

Bad:

```text
Redis key expires
  ↓
auto-cancel order permanently
```

Better:

```text
Camunda timer or DB scheduler stores due timeout
  ↓
Redis key expiration optionally wakes worker
  ↓
worker checks Camunda/PostgreSQL current state
  ↓
if still due, perform idempotent transition
```

Camunda 8 messages are discarded if no matching subscription is open, so a timeout/correlation strategy should not rely on blind message publication without understanding current workflow state/subscription availability. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/messages/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 16. Workflow Progress Cache

For UI:

```text
GET /cases/{caseId}/workflow-progress
```

Redis can cache:

```json
{
  "caseId": "c991",
  "processState": "WAITING_APPROVAL",
  "progressPercent": 60,
  "activeStep": "Supervisor Review",
  "lastEvent": "DOCUMENT_VERIFIED",
  "updatedAt": "2026-07-13T18:20:00+07:00"
}
```

Key:

```text
case-svc:prod:tenant:t1:workflow-progress:v1:case:c991
```

TTL:

```text
15 seconds - 2 minutes
```

Invalidation:

```text
Camunda/job/outbox event
  ↓
delete/update Redis progress cache
  ↓
publish WebSocket signal
```

Do not use progress cache as process state authority.

---

## 17. Workflow Event Projection

Camunda events / outbox events can update a read model.

Pattern:

```text
Camunda transition / business transaction
  ↓
PostgreSQL outbox event
  ↓
Kafka or internal dispatcher
  ↓
workflow_projection table
  ↓
Redis cache for fast UI read
```

This is better than querying Camunda engine for every UI request.

Projection table:

```sql
CREATE TABLE workflow_projections (
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    business_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    process_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    state TEXT NOT NULL,
    active_step TEXT NULL,
    version BIGINT NOT NULL,
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL,
    payload JSONB NOT NULL,

    PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, business_key, process_type)
);
```

Redis caches this projection, not the raw process truth.

---

## 18. Message Correlation Outbox

Problem:

```text
DB commit succeeds
Camunda correlate message fails
```

Use outbox:

```sql
CREATE TABLE workflow_message_outbox (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    message_name TEXT NOT NULL,
    correlation_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    business_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    payload JSONB NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    attempt_count INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    next_attempt_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    dispatched_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
    last_error TEXT NULL
);
```

Flow:

```text
PostgreSQL transaction:
  update business state
  insert workflow_message_outbox
commit

Dispatcher:
  publish/correlate message to Camunda
  mark dispatched
```

Redis can help:

```text
- dedup dispatcher attempt
- rate limit Camunda calls
- cache correlation key
- progress signal
```

But outbox is the durable retry mechanism.

---

## 19. Camunda Command Idempotency

Commands that should be idempotent:

```text
- start process
- correlate message
- complete task/job
- fail job
- cancel process
- trigger compensation
```

Pattern:

```text
operationKey = tenant + operationType + businessKey + requestHash
```

Redis:

```text
workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:idem:correlate-payment:v1:key:kHash
```

PostgreSQL:

```sql
CREATE UNIQUE INDEX uq_workflow_command_idem
ON workflow_operations (
  tenant_id,
  operation_type,
  idempotency_key_hash
);
```

Response behavior:

```text
same key + same request:
  replay result / return existing operation status

same key + different request:
  409 Conflict

processing:
  202 Accepted or 409 depending API
```

---

## 20. Redis Stream vs Camunda Job

Do not duplicate workflow engine semantics accidentally.

| Need | Better tool |
|---|---|
| BPMN service task execution | Camunda job/external task |
| Technical async job unrelated to BPMN | Redis Stream |
| Durable cross-service event | Kafka |
| User-visible workflow state | Camunda/projection table |
| Retry external side effect inside process | Camunda job retry + DB outbound state |
| Bulk background processing | Redis Stream / DB job |
| Human task lifecycle | Camunda |

If a job is part of BPMN lifecycle, prefer Camunda job/external task as primary. Redis Streams may still be used behind a worker internally, but then you need to be careful not to create two independent retry systems.

---

## 21. Combining Camunda Worker + Redis Stream

Sometimes a Camunda worker receives a job but wants to offload heavy work.

Pattern:

```text
Camunda job activated
  ↓
create DB job/outbound operation
  ↓
enqueue Redis Stream internal heavy job
  ↓
do not complete Camunda job until heavy job result is durable
```

Safer alternative:

```text
Camunda job worker performs the work directly with bounded concurrency
  ↓
fail with retry backoff if dependency unavailable
```

Risk if offloading:

```text
Camunda job timeout may occur while Redis job still running.
Another Camunda worker may pick the job.
```

If offloading is necessary:

```text
- extend/update Camunda job timeout where supported
- use durable operation table
- use idempotent Redis worker
- complete Camunda job only once
- handle Camunda completion conflict safely
```

Camunda 8 supports updating job timeout, and docs recommend not waiting until the last second to update timeout because the job may already time out before the update is processed. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/concepts/job-workers/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 22. Compensation Support Pattern

Redis can store temporary compensation hints:

```text
order-svc:prod:tenant:t1:comp-hint:v1:order:o123
```

Value:

```json
{
  "reservedInventory": true,
  "paymentAuthorized": true,
  "shipmentCreated": false,
  "cachedAt": "2026-07-13T18:30:00+07:00"
}
```

But compensation truth should be:

```text
- Camunda process variables/history
- PostgreSQL saga participant records
- external provider state
```

Redis compensation hint is only optimization for faster decision/display.

---

## 23. Workflow Locking: Use Carefully

Redis lock can be used for:

```text
- prevent two HTTP submit commands for same case
- prevent duplicate cache rebuild
- limit worker execution per aggregate
- protect external provider concurrency
```

But for workflow correctness:

```text
Camunda process/task state must still be checked.
PostgreSQL business state must still be checked.
```

Example:

```text
Redis lock acquired for approve-case
  ↓
DB checks case status WAITING_APPROVAL and version
  ↓
Camunda completes/correlates expected task/message
  ↓
outbox writes CaseApproved
```

Redis lock alone is not enough.

---

## 24. Correlation Key Design

Good correlation key:

```text
tenant:t1:case:c991
order:o123
application:a456
```

Avoid using volatile internal technical IDs as only correlation key if business recovery depends on it.

Camunda 8 docs describe correlation by message name plus correlation key, often based on a process variable such as `orderId`. ([Camunda 8 Docs](https://docs.camunda.io/docs/components/modeler/bpmn/message-events/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Recommended:

```text
correlationKey = stable business key
```

Examples:

```text
case:c991
order:o123
payment:p456
tenant:t1:case:c991
```

Store mapping:

```sql
CREATE TABLE workflow_correlations (
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    business_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    process_type TEXT NOT NULL,
    correlation_key TEXT NOT NULL,
    process_instance_id TEXT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),

    PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, business_key, process_type)
);
```

Redis caches this mapping.

---

## 25. Failure Mode Table

| Failure | Risk | Guardrail |
|---|---|---|
| Redis correlation cache miss | Slightly slower lookup | Query DB/Camunda |
| Redis stale process cache | Wrong UI/decision if trusted | Use only as projection |
| Camunda command timeout | Unknown result | Workflow operation table + reconciliation |
| Worker timeout | Duplicate processing | Idempotent worker + DB guard |
| Job retries exhausted | Incident / stuck process | Alert + runbook |
| Redis dedup expired too early | Duplicate side effect possible | DB idempotency |
| Message correlation with no subscription | Message discarded in Camunda 8 | Durable outbox + state check |
| Redis keyspace notification missed | Timeout signal lost | Camunda timer/DB scheduler |
| Redis Stream job offload runs after Camunda timeout | Duplicate worker path | Extend timeout / operation table |
| External provider timeout | Unknown side effect | Provider idempotency + reconciliation |
| Kafka event publish fails | Missing integration event | Outbox retry |

---

## 26. Java Package Boundary

```text
com.company.workflow
├── api
│   └── CaseWorkflowResource.java
├── application
│   ├── WorkflowCommandService.java
│   └── WorkflowQueryService.java
├── camunda
│   ├── WorkflowGateway.java
│   ├── Camunda7WorkflowGateway.java
│   ├── Camunda8WorkflowGateway.java
│   └── CamundaWorkerAdapter.java
├── redis
│   ├── WorkflowCorrelationCache.java
│   ├── WorkflowProgressCache.java
│   ├── WorkflowDedupStore.java
│   └── WorkflowWorkerSemaphore.java
├── persistence
│   ├── WorkflowOperationRepository.java
│   ├── WorkflowCorrelationRepository.java
│   ├── OutboxRepository.java
│   └── SagaRepository.java
└── worker
    ├── WorkflowJobHandler.java
    ├── ExternalProviderWorker.java
    └── WorkflowReconciliationWorker.java
```

Interface boundary:

```java
public interface WorkflowGateway {
    WorkflowStartResult startProcess(StartWorkflowCommand command);
    CorrelationResult correlateMessage(CorrelateMessageCommand command);
    CompletionResult completeJob(CompleteWorkflowJobCommand command);
    FailureResult failJob(FailWorkflowJobCommand command);
}
```

Application service should depend on `WorkflowGateway`, not directly on Camunda REST/gRPC client.

---

## 27. Workflow Command Service Shape

```java
public final class CaseWorkflowCommandService {

    private final IdempotencyStore idempotencyStore;
    private final TransactionRunner tx;
    private final CaseRepository caseRepository;
    private final WorkflowOperationRepository operationRepository;
    private final WorkflowGateway workflowGateway;
    private final WorkflowCorrelationCache correlationCache;

    public SubmitCaseResult submitCase(
        TenantId tenantId,
        CaseId caseId,
        UserContext user,
        String idempotencyKey
    ) {
        String requestHash = requestHasher.hash(
            "submit-case",
            tenantId,
            caseId,
            user.userId()
        );

        IdempotencyDecision idem = idempotencyStore.checkOrStart(
            tenantId,
            "submit-case",
            idempotencyKey,
            requestHash
        );

        if (idem.completed()) {
            return replaySubmitResult(idem.resultRef());
        }

        if (idem.conflict()) {
            throw new IdempotencyConflictException();
        }

        WorkflowOperation op = tx.run(() -> {
            CaseRow row = caseRepository.findForUpdate(tenantId, caseId)
                .orElseThrow(NotFoundException::new);

            domainPolicy.assertCanSubmit(row, user);

            caseRepository.markSubmitted(tenantId, caseId, row.version());

            return operationRepository.insertProcessing(
                tenantId,
                "submit-case",
                "case:" + caseId.value(),
                hash(idempotencyKey),
                requestHash
            );
        });

        WorkflowStartResult result = workflowGateway.startProcess(
            StartWorkflowCommand.caseSubmitted(tenantId, caseId, op.id())
        );

        operationRepository.markCompleted(op.id(), result.processInstanceId());

        correlationCache.put(
            tenantId,
            "case:" + caseId.value(),
            result.processInstanceId(),
            Duration.ofMinutes(10)
        );

        idempotencyStore.markCompleted(
            tenantId,
            "submit-case",
            idempotencyKey,
            requestHash,
            "workflowOperation:" + op.id()
        );

        return SubmitCaseResult.from(result);
    }
}
```

Production note:

```text
If workflowGateway.startProcess() result is unknown due to timeout,
mark operation UNKNOWN_NEEDS_RECONCILIATION and reconcile by business key.
```

---

## 28. Reconciliation Worker

Reconciliation is mandatory for workflow command uncertainty.

```text
Find workflow_operations
WHERE status IN ('PROCESSING', 'CAMUNDA_REQUESTED', 'UNKNOWN_NEEDS_RECONCILIATION')
AND updated_at < now - threshold
```

For each:

```text
check PostgreSQL business state
check Camunda by business key / operation id / correlation
  ├── process exists and state expected
  │     mark completed
  │     update Redis cache
  │
  ├── process not found and DB says should exist
  │     retry start/correlation safely
  │
  ├── process completed
  │     mark completed
  │
  └── inconsistent
        alert / manual intervention
```

Redis should not be used as reconciliation source. Redis can speed lookup only.

---

## 29. Worker Idempotency Pattern

For Camunda worker handling job:

```java
public void handle(JobContext job) {
    String dedupKey = redisKeys.workflowJobDedup(
        job.tenantId(),
        job.jobKey(),
        job.workerType()
    );

    boolean first = redisDedup.tryStart(dedupKey, Duration.ofHours(1));

    if (!first) {
        // Still check DB before deciding.
        if (processedJobRepository.isProcessed(job.jobKey())) {
            workflowGateway.completeJob(job.completeCommand());
            return;
        }
    }

    ProcessedJobRecord record = processedJobRepository.insertIfAbsent(
        job.tenantId(),
        job.jobKey(),
        job.workerType(),
        job.businessKey()
    );

    if (!record.inserted()) {
        workflowGateway.completeJob(job.completeCommand());
        return;
    }

    try {
        JobResult result = businessHandler.handle(job);
        processedJobRepository.markCompleted(job.jobKey(), result.ref());
        workflowGateway.completeJob(job.completeCommand(result.variables()));
    } catch (RetryableException ex) {
        workflowGateway.failJob(job.failCommand(ex));
    } catch (BusinessException ex) {
        workflowGateway.throwBpmnError(job.bpmnErrorCommand(ex));
    }
}
```

Key point:

```text
Redis dedup is fast.
PostgreSQL processed_job is final.
Camunda completion/failure moves workflow.
```

---

## 30. Redis Keys for Workflow

| Purpose | Key |
|---|---|
| Correlation cache | `workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:corr:v1:case:c991` |
| Progress cache | `case-svc:prod:tenant:t1:workflow-progress:v1:case:c991` |
| Job dedup | `workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:dedup:job:v1:job:jHash` |
| Worker semaphore | `workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:semaphore:worker:notice:v1` |
| Operation idempotency | `workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:idem:submit-case:v1:key:kHash` |
| WebSocket signal | `workflow-svc:prod:pubsub:workflow-progress:v1` |
| Timeout wake-up | `workflow-svc:prod:tenant:t1:wakeup:timeout:v1:case:c991` |
| Saga projection | `order-svc:prod:tenant:t1:saga-cache:v1:order:o123` |

TTL rule:

```text
Correlation/progress cache:
  short to medium TTL

Dedup:
  longer than retry window

Timeout wake-up:
  only signal; durable due state elsewhere

Semaphore:
  lease TTL

Idempotency:
  24-72h or domain-specific
```

---

## 31. Observability

Metrics:

```text
workflow.redis.correlation.hit.count
workflow.redis.correlation.miss.count
workflow.redis.progress.hit.count
workflow.redis.progress.stale.count

workflow.operation.started.count
workflow.operation.completed.count
workflow.operation.unknown.count
workflow.operation.reconciled.count
workflow.operation.failed.count

workflow.worker.job.received.count
workflow.worker.job.completed.count
workflow.worker.job.failed.count
workflow.worker.job.duplicate.count
workflow.worker.job.timeout.count

workflow.camunda.command.success.count
workflow.camunda.command.error.count
workflow.camunda.command.timeout.count

workflow.semaphore.acquire.success.count
workflow.semaphore.acquire.denied.count

workflow.outbox.pending.count
workflow.outbox.oldest_pending_age
```

Labels:

```text
service
env
workflow_type
operation_type
worker_type
result
exception_class
```

Avoid:

```text
raw processInstanceId
raw businessKey if sensitive
raw tenant ID if cardinality high
raw Redis key
idempotency key
```

## 32. Logging

Good:

```json
{
  "event": "workflow_operation_unknown",
  "operationType": "submit-case",
  "businessKeyHash": "sha256-...",
  "workflowType": "case-review",
  "reason": "camunda_command_timeout",
  "action": "reconciliation_required",
  "correlationId": "..."
}
```

Worker duplicate:

```json
{
  "event": "workflow_job_duplicate_detected",
  "workerType": "send-notice",
  "jobKeyHash": "sha256-...",
  "decision": "db_record_already_completed_complete_camunda_if_needed",
  "correlationId": "..."
}
```

Bad:

```json
{
  "businessKey": "case:real-sensitive-id",
  "payload": "full process variables with PII"
}
```

---

## 33. Testing Matrix

| Test | Tujuan |
|---|---|
| Duplicate submit same idempotency key | Process not started twice |
| Same idempotency key different payload | 409 Conflict |
| Redis idempotency lost | PostgreSQL unique still protects |
| Camunda start timeout | Operation marked unknown |
| Reconciliation finds existing process | Operation completed |
| Message correlation no subscription | Outbox/retry/state handling |
| Worker receives same job twice | DB processed_job prevents duplicate side effect |
| Worker timeout causes reassignment | Idempotent worker handles repeat |
| External provider timeout | Outbound request reconciliation |
| Semaphore denied | Worker backs off/fails retryably |
| Progress cache stale | Fallback source/projection |
| Redis down | Workflow command still safe or fails clearly |
| Outbox dispatcher down | Events remain pending |
| Camunda unavailable | Operation retryable, no business corruption |
| WebSocket signal lost | UI recovers by polling/projection |

---

## 34. Common Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Solusi |
|---|---|---|
| Redis stores workflow state as source of truth | Workflow lost/stale | Camunda/PostgreSQL owns state |
| Key expiration drives business timeout | Missed/delayed transition | Camunda timer / DB scheduler |
| Pub/Sub triggers irreversible workflow transition | Event loss risk | Durable outbox/Camunda |
| Process start without idempotency | Duplicate process | Redis + DB idempotency |
| Worker not idempotent | Duplicate side effects | DB processed job/outbound request |
| Redis dedup only | Duplicate after expiry/loss | PostgreSQL final guard |
| Correlate message blindly from Redis cache | Stale/wrong correlation | Check DB/Camunda state |
| Offload Camunda job to Redis Stream without timeout strategy | Job duplicate/timeout | Extend timeout or keep work in Camunda worker |
| Store full process variables in Redis | PII/stale/schema risk | Store projection/minimal metadata |
| No reconciliation for unknown Camunda command | Stuck/inconsistent operation | Reconciliation worker |
| Kafka/Redis event before DB commit | Phantom event | Outbox after DB transaction |

---

## 35. Senior Engineer Checklist

```text
[ ] Apakah ini workflow state, business state, atau temporary helper?
[ ] Apakah Camunda tetap menjadi owner BPMN/process lifecycle?
[ ] Apakah PostgreSQL tetap menjadi source business truth?
[ ] Apakah Redis hanya cache/helper/coordination?
[ ] Apakah process start/correlation idempotent?
[ ] Apakah idempotency punya Redis fast gate dan DB final guard?
[ ] Apakah worker idempotent terhadap duplicate delivery?
[ ] Apakah external side effect punya outbound_request dan provider idempotency key?
[ ] Apakah Camunda command timeout menghasilkan UNKNOWN + reconciliation?
[ ] Apakah message correlation memakai stable business correlation key?
[ ] Apakah timeout bisnis memakai Camunda timer/DB scheduler, bukan Redis TTL saja?
[ ] Apakah progress cache punya TTL dan fallback?
[ ] Apakah worker concurrency cap memakai semaphore/rate limiter jika perlu?
[ ] Apakah outbox dipakai untuk event/job publish setelah DB commit?
[ ] Apakah Redis failure behavior jelas?
[ ] Apakah observability mencakup operation unknown/reconciled/duplicate?
```

---

## 36. Ringkasan Praktis

```text
Redis dalam workflow/Camunda:
  - correlation cache
  - process lookup cache
  - progress projection cache
  - idempotency fast gate
  - worker dedup helper
  - semaphore/rate limiter
  - wake-up signal
  - websocket bridge

Redis bukan:
  - workflow engine
  - process state source of truth
  - durable saga state
  - business timeout authority
  - audit ledger
  - reliable cross-service event backbone

Camunda:
  - BPMN lifecycle
  - job/external task lifecycle
  - timers
  - human tasks
  - incidents/retries

PostgreSQL:
  - business state
  - idempotency final guard
  - workflow operation recovery
  - outbox
  - audit/projection

Kafka:
  - durable cross-service events
```

Kalimat kunci:

> Redis adalah akselerator dan control-plane helper untuk workflow, bukan pemilik workflow. Semua keputusan final tetap harus bisa direkonstruksi dari Camunda, PostgreSQL, outbox, dan audit trail.

# Seri 20 - Realtime Notification & WebSocket Support

Status: **Seri 20 dari 22 seri lanjutan**.  
Setelah seri ini selesai, tersisa **2 seri lagi**.

---

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas bagaimana Redis membantu realtime notification dalam aplikasi:

```text id="rt-01"
Java 17+
Jersey/JAX-RS
Jakarta WebSocket
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Redis
Kafka
Camunda
Docker / Kubernetes
```

Fokusnya:

```text id="rt-02"
- WebSocket fanout antar pod
- Redis Pub/Sub sebagai live signal
- Redis Streams untuk durable-ish notification queue
- presence / online status
- notification delivery state
- offline fallback
- websocket session registry
- backpressure
- reconnect/resume
- boundary antara realtime signal vs durable notification
```

WebSocket sendiri adalah protokol full-duplex untuk komunikasi dua arah antara client dan server setelah opening handshake di atas TCP; RFC 6455 mendefinisikan model handshake dan framing WebSocket. ([IETF Datatracker](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6455?utm_source=chatgpt.com)) Jakarta WebSocket menyediakan API Java untuk membangun aplikasi WebSocket, dan `@ServerEndpoint` mendeklarasikan class sebagai endpoint WebSocket yang tersedia pada URI tertentu. ([Jakarta EE](https://jakarta.ee/specifications/websocket/2.0/apidocs/jakarta/websocket/server/serverendpoint?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model

```text id="rt-03"
WebSocket
= persistent client connection
= bidirectional communication
= per-pod local session

Redis Pub/Sub
= live fanout signal antar pod
= at-most-once
= no replay
= subscriber offline misses message

Redis Streams
= internal notification/job queue
= consumer group
= ack/pending/retry

PostgreSQL
= durable notification state
= read/unread/delivery history
= audit if needed

Kafka
= durable cross-service event source

Camunda
= workflow event source/timer/state
```

Kalimat kunci:

> WebSocket mengirim realtime signal. PostgreSQL/Kafka/Camunda menyimpan kebenaran durable. Redis menjembatani antar pod dan mempercepat fanout, tetapi Pub/Sub tidak boleh menjadi satu-satunya record notification penting.

Redis Pub/Sub memakai delivery semantics **at-most-once**; jika subscriber tidak bisa menerima message, message tidak dikirim ulang. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/pubsub/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 3. Diagram Besar

```text id="rt-04"
Business Event
  ├── PostgreSQL transaction / outbox
  ├── Kafka domain event
  └── Camunda workflow event
        ↓
Notification Service
  ↓
PostgreSQL notification table
  ↓
Redis Pub/Sub live signal
  ↓
WebSocket pods
  ├── Pod A local sessions
  ├── Pod B local sessions
  └── Pod C local sessions
        ↓
Connected clients receive realtime message

Offline client:
  ↓
Later reconnect
  ↓
Fetch missed notifications from PostgreSQL / API
```

---

## 4. Boundary Utama

| Concern | Owner utama | Redis role |
|---|---|---|
| User connected sekarang? | WebSocket pod + Redis presence | TTL/presence helper |
| Kirim realtime ke connected user | WebSocket pod | Pub/Sub fanout |
| Notification belum dibaca | PostgreSQL | Cache optional |
| Notification harus tidak hilang | PostgreSQL/Kafka | Signal only |
| Cross-service business event | Kafka/outbox | Optional local bridge |
| Workflow update | Camunda/projection DB | Progress cache/signal |
| Offline notification | PostgreSQL/API/push provider | Queue helper |
| Retry delivery | DB job/Stream | Worker queue |

Rule:

```text id="rt-05"
If user is online:
  send realtime via WebSocket.

If user is offline:
  store durable notification and let client fetch later.

If message must not be lost:
  persist before publishing.
```

---

# Bagian A - WebSocket in Java/Jakarta

## 5. Jakarta WebSocket Endpoint

Contoh endpoint:

```java id="rt-06"
@ServerEndpoint("/ws/notifications")
public final class NotificationWebSocketEndpoint {

    @OnOpen
    public void onOpen(Session session, EndpointConfig config) {
        // authenticate, register session
    }

    @OnMessage
    public void onMessage(String message, Session session) {
        // heartbeat, client ack, subscription update, etc.
    }

    @OnClose
    public void onClose(Session session, CloseReason reason) {
        // unregister session
    }

    @OnError
    public void onError(Session session, Throwable error) {
        // log safely, close/unregister if needed
    }
}
```

Jakarta WebSocket API juga menyediakan programmatic endpoint via `Endpoint`, dengan lifecycle seperti open, error, dan close. ([Jakarta EE](https://jakarta.ee/specifications/websocket/2.0/apidocs/jakarta/websocket/endpoint?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 6. WebSocket vs REST/JAX-RS

| Aspek | REST/JAX-RS | WebSocket |
|---|---|---|
| Connection | Per request | Long-lived |
| Direction | Client request → server response | Bidirectional |
| Best for | CRUD/query/command | Realtime update |
| Auth | Per request token/session | Handshake + revalidation |
| Scaling state | Stateless lebih mudah | Session local per pod |
| Backpressure | HTTP timeout/status | Send queue/session control |
| Failure | Request gagal | Connection drop/reconnect |

Rekomendasi:

```text id="rt-07"
REST:
  command/query source of truth

WebSocket:
  realtime notification/progress signal

Client:
  on reconnect, fetch current state via REST
```

Jangan membuat WebSocket sebagai satu-satunya API untuk state penting.

---

## 7. Authentication saat WebSocket Handshake

Pilihan umum:

```text id="rt-08"
- secure cookie session
- short-lived bearer token
- one-time websocket ticket
```

Hindari:

```text id="rt-09"
- long-lived token di query string
- raw token di log
- trust userId dari client message
```

Pattern aman:

```text id="rt-10"
Client calls REST:
  POST /ws-ticket

Server:
  validates session/token
  creates short-lived ticket in Redis
  returns ticket

Client:
  connects /ws/notifications?ticket=...

WebSocket server:
  consumes ticket once using Redis SET/GETDEL/Lua
```

Ticket Redis key:

```text id="rt-11"
notif-svc:prod:tenant:t1:ws-ticket:v1:ticket:sha256-ticket
```

TTL:

```text id="rt-12"
30-120 seconds
```

Failure policy:

```text id="rt-13"
Invalid/missing/expired ticket:
  reject WebSocket handshake
```

---

## 8. WebSocket Session Registry

Setiap pod menyimpan session lokal:

```text id="rt-14"
local map:
  userIdHash -> Set<WebSocketSession>
```

Redis menyimpan global presence/index:

```text id="rt-15"
notif-svc:prod:tenant:t1:presence:v1:user:uHash:conn:cHash
```

Value:

```json id="rt-16"
{
  "podId": "notif-svc-pod-a",
  "connectionIdHash": "sha256-...",
  "userIdHash": "sha256-...",
  "connectedAt": "2026-07-13T19:00:00+07:00",
  "lastHeartbeatAt": "2026-07-13T19:01:00+07:00"
}
```

TTL:

```text id="rt-17"
30-90 seconds
```

Heartbeat refresh:

```text id="rt-18"
SET presenceKey value EX 60
```

Interpretation:

```text id="rt-19"
Presence expiry means "maybe offline",
not permanent truth.
```

---

# Bagian B - Redis Pub/Sub Fanout

## 9. Pub/Sub Fanout Pattern

Problem:

```text id="rt-20"
User u123 connected to Pod B.
Business event is produced by Pod A.
Pod A does not have u123 session locally.
```

Solution:

```text id="rt-21"
Pod A publishes Redis Pub/Sub message.
All notification pods subscribe.
Only pod with local session sends to user.
```

Channel:

```text id="rt-22"
notif-svc:prod:pubsub:user-notification:v1
```

Message:

```json id="rt-23"
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "tenantId": "t1",
  "userIdHash": "sha256-user",
  "notificationId": "n123",
  "eventType": "CASE_STATUS_CHANGED",
  "occurredAt": "2026-07-13T19:05:00+07:00"
}
```

Important:

```text id="rt-24"
Pub/Sub message should be a signal.
Notification content/state should be fetchable from durable store.
```

Redis Pub/Sub subscribers that are offline when a message is published miss it permanently; pair Pub/Sub with durable state if replay is needed. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/use-cases/pub-sub/redis-py/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 10. Pub/Sub Subscriber Architecture

```text id="rt-25"
Redis Pub/Sub dedicated connection
  ↓
NotificationSubscriber
  ↓
bounded executor
  ↓
LocalWebSocketSessionRegistry
  ↓
send message to local sessions
```

Rules:

```text id="rt-26"
- dedicated Pub/Sub connection
- do not block Redis event listener thread
- bounded send queue
- message parser validates schema
- ignore messages for users not connected locally
- no business transaction in subscriber
```

---

## 11. Pub/Sub Channel Design

Options:

### A. Single global channel

```text id="rt-27"
notif-svc:prod:pubsub:notification:v1
```

Kelebihan:

```text id="rt-28"
simple
```

Kekurangan:

```text id="rt-29"
all pods receive all messages
filtering in app
```

### B. Channel per tenant

```text id="rt-30"
notif-svc:prod:tenant:t1:pubsub:notification:v1
```

Kelebihan:

```text id="rt-31"
less irrelevant traffic per tenant subscriber if subscription model supports it
```

Kekurangan:

```text id="rt-32"
too many channels/subscriptions if many tenants
```

### C. Sharded channel

Redis 7 introduced sharded Pub/Sub in Redis Cluster; shard channels are assigned to hash slots using the same algorithm as Redis Cluster keys. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/pubsub/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Example:

```text id="rt-33"
notif-svc:prod:{tenant-group:g1}:pubsub:notification:v1
notif-svc:prod:{tenant-group:g2}:pubsub:notification:v1
```

Use sharded Pub/Sub when:

```text id="rt-34"
- Redis Cluster
- high Pub/Sub throughput
- global Pub/Sub cluster bus fanout becomes bottleneck
```

---

## 12. Pub/Sub Safety Rules

```text id="rt-35"
[ ] Message loss acceptable or recoverable
[ ] Durable notification stored before publish
[ ] Subscriber is idempotent
[ ] Client reconnect fetches missed state
[ ] Pub/Sub is not audit log
[ ] Pub/Sub is not delivery guarantee
```

Bad:

```text id="rt-36"
PUBLISH notification body
  ↓
No DB record
  ↓
Client offline
  ↓
Notification lost forever
```

Good:

```text id="rt-37"
INSERT notification row
  ↓
COMMIT
  ↓
PUBLISH notification signal
  ↓
Client receives now or fetches later
```

---

# Bagian C - Durable Notification Model

## 13. Notification Table

Untuk notification yang tidak boleh hilang:

```sql id="rt-38"
CREATE TABLE notifications (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    recipient_user_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    type TEXT NOT NULL,
    title TEXT NOT NULL,
    body TEXT NULL,
    payload JSONB NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    read_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
    expires_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL
);
```

Index:

```sql id="rt-39"
CREATE INDEX idx_notifications_inbox
ON notifications (tenant_id, recipient_user_id, created_at DESC);

CREATE INDEX idx_notifications_unread
ON notifications (tenant_id, recipient_user_id, status)
WHERE status = 'UNREAD';
```

Redis Pub/Sub signal:

```json id="rt-40"
{
  "notificationId": "uuid",
  "recipientUserHash": "sha256-...",
  "type": "CASE_ASSIGNED"
}
```

Client can fetch:

```text id="rt-41"
GET /notifications?after=<lastSeenCreatedAt>
GET /notifications/unread-count
PATCH /notifications/{id}/read
```

---

## 14. Notification Outbox

If notification is created inside business transaction:

```text id="rt-42"
BEGIN
  update case/order/payment
  insert notification row
  insert outbox event NOTIFICATION_CREATED
COMMIT
```

Dispatcher:

```text id="rt-43"
read outbox
  ↓
PUBLISH Redis Pub/Sub signal
  ↓
mark dispatched
```

This prevents:

```text id="rt-44"
DB updated but notification signal publish failed with no retry path
```

For cross-service notification event:

```text id="rt-45"
business service -> PostgreSQL outbox -> Kafka -> notification service -> notification table -> Redis Pub/Sub
```

---

## 15. Notification Delivery State

Different states:

| State | Meaning |
|---|---|
| `CREATED` | Durable row exists |
| `UNREAD` | User has not read |
| `READ` | User marked read |
| `DELIVERED_WS` | At least one websocket send attempted/succeeded |
| `FAILED_WS` | Realtime send failed |
| `EXPIRED` | Notification no longer relevant |
| `ARCHIVED` | Hidden/archived |

Important:

```text id="rt-46"
WebSocket delivered does not mean user read it.
```

Read state should be updated by explicit user action or client acknowledgement policy, not by mere Pub/Sub/WebSocket send.

---

## 16. Client ACK

You can support client ack:

```json id="rt-47"
{
  "type": "ACK_NOTIFICATION",
  "notificationId": "n123",
  "clientMessageId": "cmsg-456"
}
```

But be clear:

```text id="rt-48"
ACK_RECEIVED:
  client app received message

READ:
  user actually read/opened notification
```

Do not conflate:

```text id="rt-49"
WebSocket send success = read
```

---

# Bagian D - Reconnect and Offline Fallback

## 17. Client Reconnect Protocol

Client stores:

```text id="rt-50"
lastSeenNotificationCreatedAt
lastSeenSequence
lastReadAt
```

On reconnect:

```text id="rt-51"
1. open WebSocket
2. authenticate
3. server accepts
4. client calls REST:
   GET /notifications?after=lastSeen
5. client reconciles missed notifications
```

Alternative server push on connect:

```text id="rt-52"
on WebSocket open:
  server sends unread count / recent missed summary
```

Still use REST for full reliable fetch.

---

## 18. Sequence Number

For per-user ordered delivery:

```sql id="rt-53"
CREATE TABLE user_notification_sequences (
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    user_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    last_sequence BIGINT NOT NULL,
    PRIMARY KEY (tenant_id, user_id)
);
```

Notification row:

```sql id="rt-54"
ALTER TABLE notifications
ADD COLUMN user_sequence BIGINT NOT NULL;
```

On create:

```text id="rt-55"
increment per-user sequence in PostgreSQL transaction
insert notification with sequence
```

Client can request:

```text id="rt-56"
GET /notifications?afterSequence=123
```

This is much stronger than relying on Pub/Sub order.

---

## 19. Offline Push Fallback

For mobile/email/SMS push:

```text id="rt-57"
notification created
  ↓
if user offline or high priority:
    enqueue push job
```

Use:

```text id="rt-58"
PostgreSQL notification + delivery_jobs
Redis Stream or DB job table for push worker
```

Do not:

```text id="rt-59"
Use Pub/Sub as only push delivery queue
```

Push provider calls need:

```text id="rt-60"
- provider idempotency key if available
- delivery attempt record
- retry/backoff
- DLQ
```

---

# Bagian E - Presence

## 20. Presence Model

Presence can answer:

```text id="rt-61"
Is user probably online now?
Which pod has user's WebSocket session?
How many active devices?
```

Redis keys:

```text id="rt-62"
notif-svc:prod:tenant:t1:presence:v1:user:uHash:conn:cHash
```

Optional index:

```text id="rt-63"
notif-svc:prod:tenant:t1:presence-index:v1:user:uHash
```

Set members:

```text id="rt-64"
connectionIdHash
```

Presence heartbeat:

```text id="rt-65"
SET presenceKey value EX 60
SADD presenceIndex connectionIdHash
EXPIRE presenceIndex 120
```

Cleanup:

```text id="rt-66"
on close:
  DEL presenceKey
  SREM presenceIndex connectionIdHash
```

But if pod crashes, close handler does not run. TTL handles eventual cleanup.

---

## 21. Presence Is Approximate

Presence failure modes:

```text id="rt-67"
- connection dropped but TTL not expired yet
- pod crash leaves index member
- heartbeat delayed
- Redis timeout
- client background mobile network pause
```

Therefore:

```text id="rt-68"
online = probable online
offline = maybe offline after grace period
```

Use presence for UX and routing optimization, not legal/business decisions.

---

## 22. Keyspace Notification for Presence

Redis keyspace notification can signal expired presence keys:

```text id="rt-69"
presence key expired
  ↓
keyspace event
  ↓
subscriber re-checks whether user has other active sessions
  ↓
publish maybe-offline signal
```

But keyspace notification is Pub/Sub-based and not durable; designs must tolerate missed events. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/pubsub/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Safe pattern:

```text id="rt-70"
expired event = wake-up signal only
actual state = scan/check current presence keys
```

---

# Bagian F - Backpressure and Slow Clients

## 23. Slow Client Problem

WebSocket client may be slow:

```text id="rt-71"
server sends messages faster than client reads
  ↓
send buffer grows
  ↓
pod memory pressure
  ↓
latency / OOM risk
```

Guardrails:

```text id="rt-72"
- per-session send queue limit
- message size limit
- drop low-priority realtime messages
- close slow connection
- client fetches missed state via REST
- do not send huge payload over WebSocket
```

Message over WebSocket should be small:

```json id="rt-73"
{
  "type": "NOTIFICATION_CREATED",
  "notificationId": "n123",
  "sequence": 456
}
```

Client fetches detail via REST if needed.

---

## 24. Server Send Queue

Per session:

```text id="rt-74"
maxQueueSize = 100
maxMessageBytes = 16KB
sendTimeout = 2s
```

If queue full:

```text id="rt-75"
low priority:
  drop signal, mark needs_resync

high priority:
  close connection with "resync required"
```

Client on reconnect:

```text id="rt-76"
GET /notifications?afterSequence=lastSequence
```

This is why durable notification state matters.

---

## 25. Binary / Large Payload

Do not send large reports/files over WebSocket.

Bad:

```text id="rt-77"
WebSocket message contains PDF/report binary
```

Better:

```text id="rt-78"
WebSocket:
  REPORT_READY notification with reportId

REST/object storage:
  GET /reports/{id}/download
```

For binary upload/download, use HTTP upload/download patterns from Jersey/JAX-RS, not WebSocket unless you have a very specific streaming requirement.

---

# Bagian G - Notification Message Schema

## 26. Realtime Message Envelope

```json id="rt-79"
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "messageId": "uuid",
  "type": "NOTIFICATION_CREATED",
  "tenantId": "t1",
  "recipientUserHash": "sha256-...",
  "notificationId": "n123",
  "sequence": 456,
  "occurredAt": "2026-07-13T19:20:00+07:00",
  "traceId": "..."
}
```

Rules:

```text id="rt-80"
- include schemaVersion
- include messageId
- include notificationId/reference
- include sequence if ordered reconciliation needed
- avoid PII
- keep payload small
```

---

## 27. Event Types

```text id="rt-81"
NOTIFICATION_CREATED
NOTIFICATION_UPDATED
NOTIFICATION_READ
UNREAD_COUNT_CHANGED
WORKFLOW_PROGRESS_CHANGED
CASE_ASSIGNED
REPORT_READY
SESSION_REVOKED
FORCE_RESYNC
PING
PONG
```

Use `FORCE_RESYNC` when server detects client may have missed state:

```json id="rt-82"
{
  "type": "FORCE_RESYNC",
  "reason": "SEND_QUEUE_OVERFLOW",
  "afterSequence": 456
}
```

---

## 28. WebSocket Client Contract

Client should:

```text id="rt-83"
- authenticate connection
- maintain heartbeat
- handle reconnect
- fetch missed notifications via REST
- ignore duplicate notificationId/messageId
- support schemaVersion
- handle FORCE_RESYNC
- not assume WebSocket delivery is durable
```

Client should not:

```text id="rt-84"
- treat every socket close as logout
- trust message order globally
- rely only on WebSocket for unread state
```

---

# Bagian H - Redis Streams for Notification Workers

## 29. When Pub/Sub Is Not Enough

Use Redis Streams when:

```text id="rt-85"
- notification send must be retried
- push provider delivery needs ack/retry
- websocket send job should be distributed
- offline push needs DLQ
```

Stream key:

```text id="rt-86"
notif-svc:prod:stream:push-delivery:v1
```

Message:

```json id="rt-87"
{
  "deliveryJobId": "dj123",
  "notificationId": "n123",
  "recipientUserIdHash": "sha256-...",
  "channel": "MOBILE_PUSH"
}
```

Worker:

```text id="rt-88"
XREADGROUP
  ↓
load delivery job from PostgreSQL
  ↓
send push
  ↓
update delivery state
  ↓
XACK
```

Redis Streams support append-only entries and consumer-group consumption, unlike Pub/Sub which has no replay/ack. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/develop/data-types/streams/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 30. Notification Delivery Job Table

```sql id="rt-89"
CREATE TABLE notification_delivery_jobs (
    id UUID PRIMARY KEY,
    tenant_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    notification_id UUID NOT NULL,
    recipient_user_id TEXT NOT NULL,
    channel TEXT NOT NULL,
    status TEXT NOT NULL,
    attempt_count INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 0,
    max_attempts INT NOT NULL DEFAULT 5,
    next_attempt_at TIMESTAMPTZ NULL,
    provider_message_id TEXT NULL,
    last_error TEXT NULL,
    created_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now(),
    updated_at TIMESTAMPTZ NOT NULL DEFAULT now()
);
```

Redis Stream only carries `deliveryJobId`.

---

# Bagian I - Integration with Business Events

## 31. Case Status Changed Example

Flow:

```text id="rt-90"
Case service transaction:
  update cases
  insert outbox CaseStatusChanged
commit

Outbox dispatcher:
  publish Kafka case.events.v1

Notification service:
  consume Kafka event
  create notification row
  insert notification outbox

Notification outbox dispatcher:
  publish Redis Pub/Sub signal

WebSocket pods:
  send realtime notification to connected sessions

Client:
  receives signal or fetches missed via REST
```

Why not directly Pub/Sub from Case Service?

```text id="rt-91"
Because notification must survive subscriber downtime and service restarts.
```

---

## 32. Workflow Progress Example

Flow:

```text id="rt-92"
Camunda process moves to WAITING_APPROVAL
  ↓
worker/projection updates workflow_projections table
  ↓
Redis progress cache invalidated/updated
  ↓
Redis Pub/Sub progress signal
  ↓
WebSocket sends WORKFLOW_PROGRESS_CHANGED
```

Client then:

```text id="rt-93"
GET /cases/{caseId}/workflow-progress
```

WebSocket message is a prompt to refresh, not the only state.

---

# Bagian J - Kubernetes / Scaling Concerns

## 33. Sticky Session?

WebSocket connection stays on one pod. In Kubernetes, ingress/load balancer routes initial connection to a pod; after upgrade, the TCP connection stays with that pod.

You usually do **not** need sticky session for Pub/Sub fanout, because every pod subscribes to Redis and can receive the signal. The pod that owns the local session sends the message.

Need sticky session only if:

```text id="rt-94"
- reconnect should prefer same pod for local-only state
- app stores non-recoverable session state locally
```

Better:

```text id="rt-95"
Keep local session state minimal.
Use Redis/PostgreSQL for global/recoverable state.
```

---

## 34. Pod Shutdown

Shutdown flow:

```text id="rt-96"
1. readiness down
2. stop accepting new WebSocket handshakes
3. notify clients with SERVER_SHUTDOWN / reconnect hint if possible
4. close WebSocket sessions gracefully
5. delete presence keys or let TTL expire
6. unsubscribe Redis Pub/Sub
7. close Redis client
```

Client must reconnect and resync.

---

## 35. Multi-Region Warning

Redis Pub/Sub is not a global reliable message bus.

For multi-region realtime:

```text id="rt-97"
- region-local WebSocket fanout via Redis
- durable events via Kafka/DB/outbox
- client reconnects to nearest region
- notification fetch via replicated/read model with consistency policy
```

Do not assume cross-region Redis Pub/Sub gives durable delivery.

---

# Bagian K - Security

## 36. WebSocket Security Checklist

```text id="rt-98"
[ ] authenticate handshake
[ ] validate Origin for browser clients where applicable
[ ] use TLS
[ ] authorize subscription topics
[ ] do not trust userId/tenantId from client message
[ ] rate limit connection attempts
[ ] rate limit client messages
[ ] message size limit
[ ] heartbeat/idle timeout
[ ] no raw token in URL logs
[ ] close on auth expiry/session revocation
[ ] avoid PII in Pub/Sub/WebSocket payload
```

RFC 6455 describes WebSocket’s origin-based browser security model as part of the protocol security context. ([IETF Datatracker](https://datatracker.ietf.org/doc/html/rfc6455?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 37. Subscription Authorization

If users subscribe to specific resources:

```json id="rt-99"
{
  "type": "SUBSCRIBE",
  "resource": "case:c991"
}
```

Server must check:

```text id="rt-100"
- user belongs to tenant
- user can view case c991
- subscription count limit
- resource exists
```

Do not allow arbitrary channel names from client to map directly to Redis Pub/Sub subscriptions.

Bad:

```text id="rt-101"
client sends "subscribe": "tenant:t2:admin-events"
server subscribes blindly
```

Better:

```text id="rt-102"
client asks for resource
server authorizes
server stores local subscription mapping
Redis subscription remains server-controlled
```

---

## 38. Session Revocation

If session/token revoked:

```text id="rt-103"
Auth service:
  revoke session/token
  publish SESSION_REVOKED signal
```

WebSocket pod:

```text id="rt-104"
receives signal
  ↓
find local sessions for user/session
  ↓
close connection
```

But Pub/Sub signal might be missed. Therefore also:

```text id="rt-105"
- heartbeat revalidates session periodically
- server checks auth expiry
- client reconnect requires fresh auth
```

---

# Bagian L - Observability

## 39. Metrics

```text id="rt-106"
websocket.connection.open.count
websocket.connection.close.count
websocket.connection.active
websocket.connection.auth.failure.count
websocket.message.sent.count
websocket.message.send.failure.count
websocket.message.dropped.count
websocket.message.queue.size
websocket.heartbeat.timeout.count
websocket.reconnect.count

redis.pubsub.publish.count
redis.pubsub.publish.error.count
redis.pubsub.message.received.count
redis.pubsub.subscriber.reconnect.count
redis.pubsub.handler.error.count

presence.active_connection.count
presence.heartbeat.update.count
presence.expired.count

notification.created.count
notification.ws.delivered.count
notification.ws.failed.count
notification.offline.count
notification.unread.count
notification.resync.count
```

Labels aman:

```text id="rt-107"
service
env
message_type
channel_family
result
exception_class
```

Avoid:

```text id="rt-108"
raw userId
raw tenantId
raw notification payload
raw WebSocket ticket
raw Redis key
```

---

## 40. Logs

Good:

```json id="rt-109"
{
  "event": "websocket_message_dropped",
  "reason": "SESSION_QUEUE_FULL",
  "messageType": "NOTIFICATION_CREATED",
  "userHash": "sha256-...",
  "action": "force_resync_on_next_message",
  "correlationId": "..."
}
```

Good reconnect:

```json id="rt-110"
{
  "event": "websocket_resync_required",
  "reason": "PUBSUB_RECONNECT",
  "lastKnownSequence": 456,
  "userHash": "sha256-..."
}
```

Bad:

```json id="rt-111"
{
  "ticket": "raw-ticket",
  "accessToken": "bearer-token",
  "messageBody": "full sensitive notification"
}
```

---

# Bagian M - Testing Matrix

## 41. WebSocket Tests

| Test | Tujuan |
|---|---|
| Valid handshake accepted | Basic auth |
| Invalid/expired ticket rejected | Security |
| WebSocket open registers local session | Registry |
| Close unregisters session | Cleanup |
| Pod crash leaves presence TTL cleanup | Recovery |
| Heartbeat refreshes presence | Presence |
| Slow client queue overflow | Backpressure |
| Large message rejected | Safety |
| Auth revoked closes socket | Security |
| Reconnect fetches missed notifications | Durability |

---

## 42. Redis Pub/Sub Tests

| Test | Tujuan |
|---|---|
| Publish signal delivered to pod with session | Fanout |
| Pod without user session ignores signal | Correct routing |
| Subscriber disconnect loses Pub/Sub message | Verify design tolerates loss |
| Reconnect triggers resync | Recovery |
| Duplicate signal does not duplicate unread state | Idempotency |
| Pub/Sub Redis down | Degraded behavior |
| High publish rate | Backpressure/latency |
| Sharded Pub/Sub channel distribution | Cluster scaling |

---

## 43. Durable Notification Tests

| Test | Tujuan |
|---|---|
| Notification stored before Pub/Sub | No loss |
| Pub/Sub publish fails after DB insert | Outbox retry |
| Client offline receives on REST fetch later | Offline fallback |
| Read notification updates DB not just socket | Correct read state |
| WebSocket delivered not equal read | Semantics |
| Sequence gap triggers resync | Reliability |
| Duplicate notification event | Unique/idempotency guard |

---

# Bagian N - Common Anti-Patterns

## 44. Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Solusi |
|---|---|---|
| Pub/Sub as only notification storage | Offline users miss messages | PostgreSQL notification table |
| WebSocket send means notification read | False read state | Separate delivered/read |
| Full PII payload over Pub/Sub | Security leak | Send IDs/reference |
| No reconnect resync | Missed realtime state | REST catch-up |
| No send queue limit | Pod memory risk | Backpressure/close slow clients |
| Trust client subscription topic | Data leak | Server-side authz |
| Presence treated as exact truth | Wrong business decision | Approximate presence |
| Keyspace expiration as reliable offline event | Missed events | TTL + periodic reconciliation |
| Redis Pub/Sub for push provider retry | Lost delivery | DB job/Redis Stream |
| No outbox before publish | Lost signal after commit | Notification outbox |
| Sticky-session-only design | Hard scaling | Redis fanout + durable state |
| No session revalidation | Revoked sessions stay connected | Periodic reauth/revoke signal |

---

## 45. Senior Engineer Checklist

```text id="rt-112"
[ ] Apa yang realtime signal dan apa yang durable notification?
[ ] Apakah notification disimpan sebelum Pub/Sub?
[ ] Apakah client punya reconnect/resync protocol?
[ ] Apakah WebSocket payload kecil dan minim PII?
[ ] Apakah subscription authorization server-side?
[ ] Apakah presence dianggap approximate?
[ ] Apakah slow client/backpressure ditangani?
[ ] Apakah Pub/Sub loss sudah acceptable/recoverable?
[ ] Apakah offline fallback ada?
[ ] Apakah delivery/read state dipisah?
[ ] Apakah push/email/SMS memakai durable job?
[ ] Apakah WebSocket auth expiry/revocation ditangani?
[ ] Apakah Redis Pub/Sub connection dedicated?
[ ] Apakah metrics active connection/drop/resync ada?
[ ] Apakah Kubernetes shutdown graceful?
```

---

## 46. Ringkasan Praktis

```text id="rt-113"
WebSocket:
  realtime bidirectional connection, local per pod

Redis Pub/Sub:
  fanout live signal antar pod, at-most-once, no replay

Redis Streams:
  retryable notification/push worker queue

PostgreSQL:
  durable notification state, unread/read, sequence, delivery job

Kafka/outbox:
  durable business event to notification service

Presence:
  approximate online state via TTL heartbeat

Client:
  must reconnect and fetch missed state via REST

Security:
  authenticate handshake, authorize subscriptions, avoid PII/secret in messages
```

Kalimat kunci:

> Realtime notification yang production-ready bukan hanya “WebSocket + Redis Pub/Sub”. Desain yang aman selalu memisahkan live signal dari durable notification state, menyediakan reconnect/resync, membatasi slow client, dan menganggap Pub/Sub sebagai akselerator realtime-bukan storage atau delivery guarantee.
