# Seri 21 - Security, Multi-Tenant Isolation & Compliance

Status: **Seri 21 dari 22 seri lanjutan**.  
Setelah seri ini selesai, tersisa **1 seri lagi**.

---

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri ini membahas Redis dari sudut pandang security dan compliance untuk microservice production:

```text id="sec-01"
Java 17+
Jersey/JAX-RS
MyBatis
PostgreSQL
Redis
Docker/Kubernetes
On-Prem / AWS / Azure
Multi-tenant environment
```

Fokusnya:

```text id="sec-02"
- Redis ACL
- TLS
- network isolation
- secret handling
- tenant isolation
- namespace governance
- PII minimization
- encryption strategy
- audit boundary
- data retention
- backup/snapshot risk
- operational compliance
- production runbook
```

Redis menyediakan **ACL** untuk membatasi user connection berdasarkan command dan key yang boleh diakses. Setelah client authenticate dengan username/password, connection diasosiasikan dengan user ACL dan batasan command/key user tersebut. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/security/acl/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)) Redis juga mendukung TLS untuk enkripsi komunikasi client-server mulai Redis 6, meskipun pada Redis Open Source dukungan TLS perlu di-build/diaktifkan sesuai deployment. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/security/encryption/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model Security Redis

```text id="sec-03"
Redis bukan sekadar cache.
Redis bisa berisi:
  - session state
  - token denylist
  - OTP hash
  - authz cache
  - user permission snapshot
  - workflow correlation
  - job metadata
  - notification reference
  - temporary business state
```

Maka Redis harus diperlakukan sebagai **sensitive data store**, bukan “hanya in-memory cache”.

Kalimat kunci:

> Redis security yang baik dimulai dari asumsi bahwa key name, value, log, backup, metric label, dan Pub/Sub payload semuanya bisa menjadi jalur kebocoran data.

---

## 3. Threat Model Praktis

| Threat | Contoh | Dampak |
|---|---|---|
| Unauthorized Redis access | Redis terbuka di network internal luas | Session/token/cache bocor |
| Over-privileged app user | App cache bisa `FLUSHALL` | Outage/data loss |
| Key name leaks PII | `session:john@example.com` | PII leak di logs/monitoring |
| Value contains secret | raw OTP/token disimpan | Account takeover |
| Tenant collision | key tenant A/B tertukar | Data leak lintas tenant |
| Weak TLS/network | traffic Redis disniff | Credential/data leak |
| Backup exposure | RDB/AOF/snapshot bocor | Data-at-rest leak |
| Dangerous command | `KEYS`, `FLUSHDB`, `CONFIG` | Performance/security incident |
| Pub/Sub over-sharing | full payload PII disiarkan | Broadcast leak |
| Eviction of security keys | denylist/session hilang | revoked token accepted / logout |
| Excessive retention | OTP/session cache terlalu lama | Compliance risk |

---

## 4. Security Boundary

```text id="sec-04"
Application
  ↓
Redis client with least-privilege ACL
  ↓
TLS/network-restricted Redis endpoint
  ↓
Redis key namespace per service/env/tenant/purpose
  ↓
Value minimized/encrypted/hashed if needed
  ↓
Metrics/logs redact sensitive material
  ↓
Audit/security events persisted outside Redis
```

Boundary per system:

```text id="sec-05"
Redis:
  temporary state / cache / coordination

PostgreSQL:
  business truth, audit, durable security event

Kafka/SIEM:
  security/audit event pipeline

Kubernetes / Cloud IAM:
  secret distribution and network isolation

Application:
  encryption, hashing, validation, authorization
```

---

# Bagian A - Redis ACL & Least Privilege

## 5. Redis ACL Concept

Redis ACL membatasi:

```text id="sec-06"
- user identity
- allowed commands
- denied commands
- allowed key patterns
- Pub/Sub channel patterns
```

Redis ACL dirancang untuk membatasi command dan key yang bisa diakses oleh connection tertentu setelah authenticate. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/security/acl/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)) Redis command reference juga menyediakan command ACL seperti `ACL CAT`, `ACL DRYRUN`, `ACL GENPASS`, dan lain-lain untuk mengelola/menguji ACL. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 6. ACL Per Service

Jangan satu Redis password dipakai semua service.

Bad:

```text id="sec-07"
all services:
  username = default
  password = same-super-password
  commands = all
  keys = *
```

Better:

```text id="sec-08"
case-svc user:
  can access case-svc:* keys only

auth-svc user:
  can access auth-svc:* and authz-svc:* specific keys

notification-svc user:
  can access notif-svc:* keys/channels

job-svc user:
  can access job-svc:* stream/list/zset keys
```

Goal:

```text id="sec-09"
If one service credential leaks,
blast radius is limited.
```

---

## 7. ACL Command Restriction

Untuk app biasa, batasi dangerous/admin commands:

```text id="sec-10"
Deny:
  FLUSHALL
  FLUSHDB
  CONFIG
  SHUTDOWN
  DEBUG
  MODULE
  ACL
  SAVE
  BGSAVE
  KEYS
  MIGRATE
  RESTORE
```

Catatan:

```text id="sec-11"
KEYS bukan hanya security risk,
tapi juga performance risk di production.
Gunakan SCAN dengan batasan dan runbook.
```

Role contoh:

| Role | Commands |
|---|---|
| Cache read-write | `GET`, `SET`, `MGET`, `DEL/UNLINK`, `EXPIRE`, `TTL`, Hash commands |
| Rate limiter | `EVALSHA`, `INCR`, `EXPIRE`, `GET`, `SET`, `Z*` relevant |
| Stream worker | `XADD`, `XREADGROUP`, `XACK`, `XPENDING`, `XAUTOCLAIM`, `XGROUP` limited |
| Pub/Sub | `PUBLISH`, `SUBSCRIBE`, `PSUBSCRIBE` channel-limited |
| Read-only dashboard | `GET`, `HGET`, `XLEN`, `XINFO`, `INFO` carefully |
| Admin/operator | Restricted to ops context, not app runtime |

---

## 8. ACL Key Pattern Restriction

Key pattern:

```text id="sec-12"
case-svc:prod:*
auth-svc:prod:*
notif-svc:prod:*
```

Better with service/env boundary:

```text id="sec-13"
~case-svc:prod:*
~case-svc:staging:*
```

Avoid:

```text id="sec-14"
~*
```

Dalam Redis ACL, key pattern restriction membantu membatasi key yang bisa diakses user tertentu. Ini penting di Redis yang dipakai bersama beberapa aplikasi/service/tenant. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/security/acl/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 9. ACL Pub/Sub Channel Restriction

Jika service memakai Pub/Sub:

```text id="sec-15"
notif-svc:prod:pubsub:*
case-svc:prod:pubsub:*
```

Batasi channel publish/subscribe.

Risk:

```text id="sec-16"
service A subscribe ke channel service B
  ↓
data leakage / unauthorized signal
```

Rule:

```text id="sec-17"
Channel name juga bagian dari security boundary.
```

---

## 10. ACL DRYRUN / Validation

Redis menyediakan command untuk mensimulasikan execution permission user tanpa menjalankan command, yaitu `ACL DRYRUN`. Command reference Redis mencantumkan `ACL DRYRUN` sebagai command yang mensimulasikan eksekusi command oleh user tanpa menjalankannya. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Gunakan saat CI/CD atau pre-prod:

```text id="sec-18"
ACL DRYRUN case-svc GET case-svc:prod:tenant:t1:cache:...
ACL DRYRUN case-svc FLUSHALL
ACL DRYRUN case-svc GET auth-svc:prod:...
```

Expected:

```text id="sec-19"
case-svc GET own key:
  allowed

case-svc FLUSHALL:
  denied

case-svc GET auth-svc key:
  denied
```

---

# Bagian B - TLS & Network Isolation

## 11. Redis TLS

Gunakan TLS untuk koneksi app → Redis, terutama jika:

```text id="sec-20"
- traffic melewati network shared
- cloud managed Redis
- multi-tenant infra
- compliance requires encryption in transit
- Redis menyimpan session/token/authz/security state
```

Redis Open Source mendukung SSL/TLS mulai Redis 6 sebagai optional feature yang perlu diaktifkan saat build/deployment. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/security/encryption/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)) Untuk Redis Cloud/managed offerings, TLS biasanya menjadi setting database/endpoint dan sebaiknya diaktifkan untuk komunikasi aplikasi-Redis jika tersedia. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/rc/security/database-security/tls-ssl/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 12. TLS Client Configuration

Yang perlu diperhatikan di Java client:

```text id="sec-21"
- TLS enabled
- hostname verification
- truststore / CA certificate
- mTLS jika digunakan
- cert rotation
- timeout tetap pendek
- no "trust all certificates" di production
```

Bad:

```java id="sec-22"
sslContext = trustAllCertificates();
hostnameVerification = false;
```

Good:

```text id="sec-23"
Use trusted CA / managed Redis CA
Enable hostname verification
Rotate credentials/certs via secret management
```

---

## 13. Network Isolation

Redis harus berada di private network.

```text id="sec-24"
Kubernetes:
  NetworkPolicy
  Service internal only
  no public LoadBalancer

Cloud:
  private endpoint / VPC / VNet
  security group / firewall allowlist
  no public internet exposure

On-prem:
  firewall segmentation
  app subnet only
  operator access via bastion/VPN
```

Golden rule:

```text id="sec-25"
Redis should never be casually reachable from every pod, laptop, or subnet.
```

---

## 14. Defense in Depth

```text id="sec-26"
Network allowlist:
  who can connect

TLS:
  protect traffic

ACL:
  what authenticated user can do

Key namespace:
  what data belongs to whom

App validation:
  what action is authorized

Audit:
  what happened
```

Jangan mengandalkan satu lapisan.

---

# Bagian C - Secret Handling

## 15. Redis Credentials

Redis credentials harus disimpan sebagai secret, bukan config biasa.

```text id="sec-27"
Kubernetes Secret / external secret manager
AWS Secrets Manager / Parameter Store
Azure Key Vault
Vault
On-prem secret management
```

Jangan:

```text id="sec-28"
- commit password ke GitHub
- taruh password di Dockerfile
- log Redis URI lengkap
- expose env var di debug endpoint
```

---

## 16. Redis URI Redaction

Bad log:

```text id="sec-29"
redis://case-svc:supersecret@redis.prod.internal:6379
```

Good log:

```text id="sec-30"
redis://case-svc:****@redis.prod.internal:6379
```

In code:

```java id="sec-31"
public String safeRedisEndpoint(RedisConfig config) {
    return "redis://" + config.username() + ":****@" + config.host() + ":" + config.port();
}
```

---

## 17. Credential Rotation

Rotation plan:

```text id="sec-32"
1. Create new Redis ACL user/password
2. Deploy app supporting new credential
3. Verify traffic using new credential
4. Disable old credential
5. Remove old secret
6. Audit no old connections remain
```

Avoid big-bang rotation without overlap unless system can tolerate outage.

---

# Bagian D - Tenant Isolation

## 18. Tenant Isolation Levels

| Level | Isolation strength | Cost |
|---|---:|---:|
| Shared Redis, key prefix tenant | Low-medium | Low |
| Shared Redis, ACL per service/env | Medium | Medium |
| Redis DB index per tenant | Limited; not cluster-friendly | Medium |
| Separate Redis instance per tenant group | Stronger | Higher |
| Dedicated Redis per tenant | Strongest | Highest |

Redis logical DB index is not a strong multi-tenant security boundary. Prefer explicit key namespace + ACL + network/service boundary; for strict tenants, use separate instances/clusters.

---

## 19. Tenant Key Namespace

Pattern:

```text id="sec-33"
{service}:{env}:tenant:{tenantIdHash}:{purpose}:{domain}:v{version}:{idHash}
```

Example:

```text id="sec-34"
case-svc:prod:tenant:tHash:cache:case-summary:v1:case:cHash
auth-svc:prod:tenant:tHash:session:v1:sid:sHash
notif-svc:prod:tenant:tHash:presence:v1:user:uHash:conn:cHash
```

Why hash tenant/user/entity IDs?

```text id="sec-35"
- avoid PII/internal ID leakage
- reduce accidental exposure in logs/metrics
- prevent easy tenant enumeration
```

---

## 20. Cross-Tenant Safety

Every Redis operation should include tenant context where applicable.

Bad:

```text id="sec-36"
GET authz:user:u123
```

Better:

```text id="sec-37"
GET authz-svc:prod:tenant:tHash:authz-snapshot:v1:user:uHash:policy:p17
```

Test for:

```text id="sec-38"
tenant A cannot read tenant B cache
tenant A cache invalidation cannot delete tenant B keys
tenant A Pub/Sub message cannot be received as tenant B event
```

---

## 21. Tenant Isolation in Pub/Sub

Bad channel:

```text id="sec-39"
notifications
```

Better:

```text id="sec-40"
notif-svc:prod:tenant-group:g1:pubsub:notification:v1
```

Payload must include tenant hash/ID and subscriber must verify expected tenant.

Rule:

```text id="sec-41"
Never rely on channel name alone.
Validate tenant in message payload too.
```

---

# Bagian E - PII Minimization & Value Security

## 22. Key Name Is Data

Key names often appear in:

```text id="sec-42"
- logs
- metrics
- slowlog
- debug output
- monitoring dashboards
- incident screenshots
- backup scans
- support tickets
```

Therefore key names must not contain:

```text id="sec-43"
- email
- phone
- national ID
- raw account number
- raw API key
- raw session ID
- raw token
- full customer name
```

---

## 23. Hash/HMAC Identifiers

For low-entropy identifiers like phone/email, prefer HMAC over plain hash.

```text id="sec-44"
idHash = HMAC-SHA256(appSecret, tenantId + ":" + identifier)
```

Why:

```text id="sec-45"
Plain SHA-256(email) can be dictionary-guessed.
HMAC requires server secret.
```

OWASP Key Management guidance emphasizes key lifecycle, storage, compromise, recovery, and destruction as explicit practices for cryptographic key management. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Key_Management_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 24. Value Minimization

Do not store full object if reference is enough.

Bad:

```json id="sec-46"
{
  "userEmail": "john@example.com",
  "phone": "+62812...",
  "fullName": "John Doe",
  "rawToken": "...",
  "permissions": "...",
  "unneededFields": "..."
}
```

Better:

```json id="sec-47"
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "userIdHash": "hmac-...",
  "policyVersion": "p17",
  "resultRef": "authzSnapshot:123",
  "cachedAt": "..."
}
```

General rule:

```text id="sec-48"
Store only what is required for the Redis use case.
Store reference if durable source can be queried.
```

---

## 25. Encryption at Application Layer

Use application-level encryption when Redis must store sensitive value and infrastructure encryption is not enough.

Use cases:

```text id="sec-49"
- sensitive workflow correlation payload
- PII-heavy temporary cache
- high-risk authz/session metadata
- tenant-specific secret-like temporary data
```

But prefer not storing sensitive value at all.

OWASP Cryptographic Storage Cheat Sheet recommends identifying what data needs protection, minimizing stored sensitive data, and using appropriate cryptographic storage design rather than treating encryption as a universal fix. ([OWASP Cheat Sheet Series](https://cheatsheetseries.owasp.org/cheatsheets/Cryptographic_Storage_Cheat_Sheet.html?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 26. Encryption Trade-Offs

| Benefit | Cost |
|---|---|
| Protects value if Redis dump leaked | Key management complexity |
| Reduces operator visibility | Harder debugging |
| Useful for backups/snapshots | Rotation/re-encryption problem |
| Defense in depth | Performance overhead |

Encryption does not protect:

```text id="sec-50"
- key names
- TTL existence pattern
- access frequency
- Pub/Sub channel names
- metadata leakage
```

So still minimize key/value metadata.

---

## 27. Secret Data That Should Not Be in Redis Plaintext

```text id="sec-51"
- password
- OTP plaintext
- refresh token plaintext
- API key plaintext
- access token plaintext
- private key
- payment card data
- national ID / sensitive documents
```

If absolutely required:

```text id="sec-52"
- store hash/HMAC/reference
- encrypt value
- short TTL
- access-controlled Redis user
- durable audit outside Redis
- documented risk acceptance
```

---

# Bagian F - Retention, Expiry, Eviction

## 28. TTL as Compliance Policy

TTL is not just memory optimization. TTL is compliance and risk policy.

| Data family | Typical TTL |
|---|---:|
| OTP | 3-10 min |
| Nonce | 5-15 min |
| Rate limit key | seconds-hours |
| Session idle | 15-60 min |
| Token denylist | until token expiry + buffer |
| Authz snapshot | 30 sec-5 min |
| Workflow progress cache | 15 sec-5 min |
| Job dedup | retry window |
| Notification presence | 30-90 sec |
| Cache DTO | minutes-hours depending data |
| Pub/Sub message | no retention |

Rule:

```text id="sec-53"
Every Redis key family must have:
  owner
  purpose
  data classification
  TTL/retention
  invalidation policy
  failure policy
```

---

## 29. Eviction Policy Risk

Redis used as cache may evict keys. That can be fine for cache DTO.

But for security/control state:

```text id="sec-54"
session key evicted:
  user logged out unexpectedly

token denylist evicted:
  revoked token may be accepted

idempotency key evicted:
  duplicate command risk

lock/semaphore key evicted:
  coordination error

stream/job key evicted:
  job loss risk
```

Recommendation:

```text id="sec-55"
Separate Redis workloads:
  - cache Redis
  - security/session Redis
  - queue/stream Redis
  - coordination Redis
```

At minimum, do not run security/queue/coordination keys on an aggressively evicting cache instance.

---

## 30. RDB/AOF/Backup Risk

Redis persistence and backup can contain sensitive data.

Think about:

```text id="sec-56"
- RDB snapshot encryption
- AOF file access
- backup retention
- backup transport
- operator access
- restore into lower environment
- data masking before restore
```

Never restore production Redis snapshot containing session/token/PII into dev/staging without explicit sanitization and approval.

---

## 31. Data Classification

Classify Redis key family:

| Class | Example | Control |
|---|---|---|
| Public/low sensitivity | feature flag cache | normal TTL |
| Internal | service config cache | ACL/TLS |
| Confidential | user profile cache | minimized/encrypted if needed |
| Security-sensitive | session/token/OTP/authz | separate Redis/strict ACL/TLS/noeviction |
| Compliance/audit | security events | not Redis primary |

---

# Bagian G - Audit & Compliance Boundary

## 32. Redis Is Not Audit Ledger

Do not use Redis as primary audit store.

Audit events:

```text id="sec-57"
- login success/failure
- token revoked
- OTP failed/locked
- admin permission changed
- case approved/rejected
- workflow completed
- notification delivery final
- data export requested
```

Store in:

```text id="sec-58"
PostgreSQL audit table
Kafka audit/security topic
SIEM/log pipeline
```

Redis can cache or rate-limit. Redis should not be the only audit record.

---

## 33. Audit Event Pattern

```text id="sec-59"
Application action
  ↓
PostgreSQL transaction
  ├── business change
  ├── audit record
  └── outbox event
  ↓
Kafka/SIEM dispatcher
```

Redis involvement:

```text id="sec-60"
- rate limit audit-heavy action
- cache actor permission
- temporary idempotency key
```

---

## 34. Compliance Questions for Redis

```text id="sec-61"
[ ] What data classes are stored in Redis?
[ ] Does Redis contain PII?
[ ] Does Redis contain authentication/session/token state?
[ ] Are Redis backups encrypted and access controlled?
[ ] What is TTL/retention per key family?
[ ] Can data subject deletion be propagated to Redis?
[ ] Is production Redis restored into non-prod?
[ ] Who can run admin commands?
[ ] Are dangerous commands restricted?
[ ] Are Redis credentials rotated?
[ ] Are logs/metrics free of PII/token/key leaks?
```

---

# Bagian H - Operational Hardening

## 35. Dangerous Commands Governance

Block or tightly restrict:

```text id="sec-62"
FLUSHALL
FLUSHDB
CONFIG
DEBUG
SHUTDOWN
MODULE
ACL
SAVE
BGSAVE
KEYS
EVAL/EVALSHA if not needed by role
SCRIPT FLUSH
```

If app needs Lua:

```text id="sec-63"
Allow EVALSHA only where possible.
Centralize scripts.
Do not let arbitrary user input become script source.
```

---

## 36. Slowlog & Monitoring Privacy

Redis slowlog or APM may capture command arguments, depending tooling.

Risk:

```text id="sec-64"
SET sessionKey {"userEmail":"..."}
```

Mitigation:

```text id="sec-65"
- no PII in key
- minimize value
- redact logs/APM
- avoid raw token arguments
- encrypt sensitive value
- use hashed identifiers
```

---

## 37. Pub/Sub Payload Security

Bad:

```json id="sec-66"
{
  "userEmail": "john@example.com",
  "caseDetails": "...",
  "token": "..."
}
```

Good:

```json id="sec-67"
{
  "schemaVersion": 1,
  "eventType": "CASE_UPDATED",
  "tenantHash": "hmac-...",
  "entityHash": "hmac-...",
  "notificationId": "uuid"
}
```

Rule:

```text id="sec-68"
Pub/Sub carries signal/reference,
not sensitive business payload.
```

---

## 38. SCAN/Delete Runbook

Mass deletion is dangerous.

Runbook:

```text id="sec-69"
1. Identify key family
2. Dry run SCAN count/sample
3. Confirm owner/team
4. Confirm tenant/env
5. Estimate number and memory
6. Use SCAN + UNLINK in batches
7. Rate limit deletion
8. Monitor Redis latency/memory
9. Record change/audit
```

Never run:

```text id="sec-70"
KEYS *
DEL huge set synchronously
FLUSHDB in shared env
```

---

## 39. Incident Response: Suspected Redis Credential Leak

Steps:

```text id="sec-71"
1. Identify affected Redis ACL user
2. Rotate credential immediately
3. Disable leaked credential
4. Inspect Redis logs/connection history if available
5. Review ACL blast radius
6. Invalidate affected sessions/tokens if needed
7. Revoke/rotate app secrets used for HMAC/encryption if exposed
8. Review backups/logs for leak propagation
9. Post-incident tighten ACL/network
```

If Redis held session/token state:

```text id="sec-72"
Assume possible session compromise.
Plan forced logout / token revocation depending blast radius.
```

---

# Bagian I - Java Client Security

## 40. Configuration Object

```java id="sec-73"
public record RedisSecurityConfig(
    boolean tlsEnabled,
    String username,
    String passwordSecretRef,
    boolean hostnameVerificationEnabled,
    String trustStoreRef,
    Duration connectTimeout,
    Duration commandTimeout
) {}
```

Rules:

```text id="sec-74"
- no password in toString()
- config logs redact secret
- TLS required in prod profile
- username required; avoid default user if possible
```

---

## 41. Safe toString

```java id="sec-75"
@Override
public String toString() {
    return "RedisSecurityConfig{" +
        "tlsEnabled=" + tlsEnabled +
        ", username='" + username + '\'' +
        ", password='****'" +
        ", hostnameVerificationEnabled=" + hostnameVerificationEnabled +
        ", connectTimeout=" + connectTimeout +
        ", commandTimeout=" + commandTimeout +
        '}';
}
```

---

## 42. Application-level Encryption Interface

```java id="sec-76"
public interface SensitiveValueCrypto {
    String encrypt(String plaintext, EncryptionContext context);
    String decrypt(String ciphertext, EncryptionContext context);
}
```

Context:

```java id="sec-77"
public record EncryptionContext(
    String tenantId,
    String keyFamily,
    String schemaVersion
) {}
```

Usage:

```java id="sec-78"
String ciphertext = crypto.encrypt(
    jsonValue,
    new EncryptionContext(tenantId, "authz-snapshot", "v1")
);

redis.setex(key, ttlSeconds, ciphertext);
```

---

## 43. HMAC Identifier Utility

```java id="sec-79"
public final class RedisIdentifierHasher {

    private final Mac mac;

    public String hmacIdentifier(String tenantId, String identifier) {
        String normalized = tenantId + ":" + identifier.trim().toLowerCase(Locale.ROOT);
        byte[] digest = mac.doFinal(normalized.getBytes(StandardCharsets.UTF_8));
        return Base64.getUrlEncoder().withoutPadding().encodeToString(digest);
    }
}
```

Careful:

```text id="sec-80"
Normalize only identifiers where normalization is semantically correct.
Do not lowercase arbitrary token/idempotency key unless contract says so.
```

---

# Bagian J - Kubernetes / Cloud / On-Prem Notes

## 44. Kubernetes

```text id="sec-81"
- Store Redis password/cert in Secret or external secret system
- Use NetworkPolicy to restrict access
- Use TLS-enabled Redis endpoint
- Do not expose Redis Service publicly
- Restrict debug pods/tools
- Avoid putting Redis credentials in ConfigMap
- Rotate secrets with rolling deploy
```

---

## 45. AWS / Azure Managed Redis

Typical controls:

```text id="sec-82"
- private endpoint / VPC/VNet integration
- TLS enabled
- auth/ACL configured
- security group/firewall rules
- backup encryption
- access audit logs where available
- no public access
```

Check provider-specific feature names and limitations because managed Redis security behavior differs by service tier.

---

## 46. On-Prem

```text id="sec-83"
- VLAN/firewall segmentation
- TLS if crossing untrusted segments
- local OS hardening
- restricted operator access
- backup encryption
- separate Redis per environment
- no shared prod/non-prod
```

---

# Bagian K - Testing & Validation

## 47. Security Test Matrix

| Test | Expected |
|---|---|
| App user cannot `FLUSHALL` | Denied |
| App user cannot access other service key | Denied |
| App user cannot access other env key | Denied |
| Wrong password rejected | Denied |
| TLS required in prod | Connection fails without TLS |
| Hostname verification on | Bad cert rejected |
| Pub/Sub channel unauthorized | Denied |
| Raw PII not present in keys | Pass scan/sample |
| Raw token not logged | Pass log scan |
| Redis down for session | Fail-closed |
| Redis down for cache | Fallback DB |
| Denylist key eviction scenario | Alert/fail policy |
| Mass delete dry run | No data modified |
| Credential rotation | No downtime |
| Backup restore non-prod | Sanitized or blocked |

---

## 48. Compliance Review Checklist

```text id="sec-84"
[ ] Data inventory per Redis key family exists
[ ] Data classification per key family exists
[ ] Owner per key family exists
[ ] TTL/retention documented
[ ] Security-sensitive keys separated or protected from eviction
[ ] ACL user per service exists
[ ] Dangerous commands restricted
[ ] TLS enabled where required
[ ] Network access restricted
[ ] Secrets stored in secret manager
[ ] Credentials rotation procedure exists
[ ] Key names avoid PII/secrets
[ ] Values minimized/encrypted/hashed where needed
[ ] Audit events stored outside Redis
[ ] Backup/snapshot risk handled
[ ] Non-prod restore policy documented
[ ] Incident response runbook exists
```

---

# Bagian L - Common Anti-Patterns

## 49. Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Solusi |
|---|---|---|
| Redis treated as harmless cache | Sensitive data leak | Data classification |
| Single default user for all apps | Huge blast radius | ACL per service |
| `~* +@all` in production app | Dangerous commands possible | Least privilege |
| No TLS over shared network | Traffic sniffing | Enable TLS/private network |
| Public Redis endpoint | Severe breach risk | Private endpoint/firewall |
| PII in key names | Logs/metrics leak | HMAC identifiers |
| Raw token/OTP in value | Account takeover | Hash/reference/encrypt |
| Security state on evictable cache Redis | Session/revocation lost | Separate/noeviction |
| Pub/Sub full payload PII | Broadcast leak | Signal/reference only |
| Redis backup to non-prod | Data leak | Sanitize/block |
| No TTL catalog | Retention drift | Key registry |
| No rotation plan | Long-lived leaked credential | Rotation runbook |
| `KEYS *` in prod | Redis stall | SCAN/runbook |
| Audit only in Redis | Audit loss | DB/Kafka/SIEM |

---

## 50. Senior Engineer Checklist

```text id="sec-85"
[ ] Redis key family apa saja yang dipakai service ini?
[ ] Apakah setiap key family punya owner, TTL, classification?
[ ] Apakah key/value mengandung PII atau secret?
[ ] Apakah Redis ACL user service ini least privilege?
[ ] Apakah dangerous commands diblokir?
[ ] Apakah TLS/network isolation aktif?
[ ] Apakah credential disimpan dan dirotasi dengan benar?
[ ] Apakah tenant ID selalu masuk key untuk tenant-scoped data?
[ ] Apakah tenant/user/entity identifier di-hash/HMAC?
[ ] Apakah security/session/token keys protected from eviction?
[ ] Apakah Pub/Sub payload hanya signal/reference?
[ ] Apakah Redis backup/snapshot aman?
[ ] Apakah audit durable di luar Redis?
[ ] Apakah failure policy Redis berbeda untuk cache vs security?
[ ] Apakah log/metric bebas raw key/token/PII?
[ ] Apakah incident runbook credential leak tersedia?
```

---

## 51. Ringkasan Praktis

```text id="sec-86"
Redis ACL:
  least privilege per service, command, key, channel

TLS/network:
  encrypt traffic, private endpoint, firewall/NetworkPolicy

Tenant isolation:
  namespace tenant-aware, HMAC identifiers, avoid cross-tenant access

PII minimization:
  key names are data; values must be minimal

Encryption:
  use application-level encryption when sensitive value must be cached

Retention:
  TTL per key family is compliance policy

Eviction:
  separate cache from security/queue/coordination workloads

Audit:
  Redis is not audit ledger; use PostgreSQL/Kafka/SIEM

Operations:
  restrict dangerous commands, handle backups, rotate credentials, test ACL
```

Kalimat kunci:

> Redis production security bukan hanya password. Security Redis mencakup ACL least privilege, TLS, network isolation, tenant-aware namespace, PII minimization, retention, backup control, failure policy, dan audit boundary yang jelas.

# Seri 22 - Production Operations: Docker, Kubernetes, HA, Monitoring, Failover

Status: **Seri 22 dari 22 seri lanjutan**.  
Dengan ini, rangkaian **Java Redis in Jersey/JAX-RS based microservice application** selesai.

---

## 1. Tujuan Seri Ini

Seri terakhir ini membahas Redis dari sisi operasi production:

```text id="ops-01"
- Docker/local development
- Kubernetes deployment concerns
- managed Redis vs self-managed Redis
- Sentinel
- Redis Cluster
- persistence: RDB/AOF/no persistence
- backup/restore
- readiness/liveness
- memory policy
- latency monitoring
- slowlog
- metrics
- failover behavior
- Java client timeout/retry
- incident runbook
```

Redis production operation harus dimulai dari klasifikasi workload. Redis untuk cache, session, queue, lock, rate limiter, dan stream punya risiko operasional yang berbeda. Redis persistence mendukung beberapa mode: **RDB snapshot**, **AOF append-only file**, atau **no persistence**; RDB memberi point-in-time snapshot, AOF mencatat write operation untuk replay saat startup, dan no persistence kadang dipakai untuk cache murni. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/persistence/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 2. Mental Model Production Redis

```text id="ops-02"
Redis production design =
  workload classification
  +
  availability target
  +
  durability target
  +
  memory/eviction policy
  +
  client timeout/retry policy
  +
  observability
  +
  failover/runbook
```

Kalimat kunci:

> Redis production bukan hanya “jalan di container”. Redis production berarti Anda tahu data apa yang boleh hilang, apa yang tidak boleh hilang, bagaimana failover terjadi, dan bagaimana aplikasi Java bereaksi saat Redis lambat/down.

---

## 3. Workload Classification

| Redis workload | Contoh | Redis boleh kehilangan data? | Persistence | Eviction |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Pure cache | DTO cache, query cache | Ya | No persistence biasanya cukup | Evictable |
| Security/session | session, token denylist, OTP | Tergantung; biasanya sensitif | Pertimbangkan AOF/managed durability | Jangan aggressive eviction |
| Coordination | lock, semaphore, leader lease | Key hilang bisa berbahaya | Biasanya no persistence okay, tapi noeviction penting | Jangan evict |
| Rate limiter | counter, bucket | Biasanya boleh hilang terbatas | No persistence sering cukup | Bisa evict jika risk accepted |
| Idempotency | command replay state | Untuk command penting jangan Redis-only | Redis + DB final guard | Jangan evict sebelum TTL |
| Job queue/List/Stream | worker queue | Jika penting, jangan Redis-only | AOF/replication + DB backing | Jangan evict |
| Pub/Sub | live signal | Ya | N/A | N/A |
| Keyspace notification | signal | Ya | N/A | N/A |

Rule:

```text id="ops-03"
Cache Redis boleh dioptimalkan untuk memory/eviction.
Security/queue/coordination Redis harus dioperasikan lebih defensif.
```

---

## 4. Deployment Options

| Option | Cocok untuk | Kelebihan | Risiko |
|---|---|---|---|
| Local Docker Redis | Dev/test | Cepat, sederhana | Bukan HA |
| Self-managed VM/on-prem | Kontrol penuh | Bisa disesuaikan | Ops burden tinggi |
| Kubernetes StatefulSet | K8s-native | Automatable | Stateful workload kompleks |
| Redis Sentinel | Non-cluster HA | Failover primary-replica | Tidak sharding |
| Redis Cluster | Sharding + HA | Scale horizontal | Multi-key complexity |
| Managed Redis AWS/Azure/etc | Production umum | Ops HA/backup/security dibantu provider | Provider-specific limit/cost |
| Separate Redis per workload | Security/ops kuat | Blast radius kecil | Biaya/kompleksitas naik |

Rekomendasi praktis:

```text id="ops-04"
Dev:
  Docker Redis

Staging:
  Similar topology to prod if possible

Production simple HA:
  Managed Redis primary/replica or Sentinel

Production scale/sharding:
  Managed Redis Cluster or self-managed Cluster

Security/session/queue:
  Prefer managed HA or dedicated Redis workload
```

---

# Bagian A - Docker & Local Development

## 5. Local Docker Redis

Untuk local dev:

```bash id="ops-05"
docker run --rm \
  --name redis-dev \
  -p 6379:6379 \
  redis:latest
```

Dengan password sederhana untuk local:

```bash id="ops-06"
docker run --rm \
  --name redis-dev \
  -p 6379:6379 \
  redis:latest \
  redis-server --requirepass local-dev-password
```

Untuk local persistence test:

```bash id="ops-07"
docker run --rm \
  --name redis-dev \
  -p 6379:6379 \
  -v redis-data:/data \
  redis:latest \
  redis-server --appendonly yes
```

Catatan:

```text id="ops-08"
Local Docker hanya untuk development.
Jangan menyimpulkan HA/failover behavior dari satu container Redis.
```

---

## 6. Docker Compose for App + Redis

```yaml id="ops-09"
services:
  redis:
    image: redis:latest
    command: ["redis-server", "--appendonly", "yes"]
    ports:
      - "6379:6379"
    volumes:
      - redis-data:/data

  app:
    build: .
    environment:
      REDIS_HOST: redis
      REDIS_PORT: "6379"
      REDIS_COMMAND_TIMEOUT_MS: "200"
    depends_on:
      - redis

volumes:
  redis-data:
```

Use local compose for:

```text id="ops-10"
- integration test
- manual debugging
- local queue/stream behavior
- TTL behavior
```

Do not use compose topology as production architecture.

---

# Bagian B - Kubernetes Runtime Concerns

## 7. Self-Managed Redis in Kubernetes

Redis in Kubernetes biasanya butuh:

```text id="ops-11"
- StatefulSet
- PersistentVolumeClaim if persistence enabled
- headless service for stable network identity
- PodDisruptionBudget
- resource requests/limits
- anti-affinity across nodes/zones
- backup job
- monitoring exporter
- security context
- NetworkPolicy
- secret management
```

Stateful Redis di Kubernetes lebih sulit daripada stateless app karena storage, failover, dan split-brain behavior harus ditangani.

Rule:

```text id="ops-12"
Jika tim belum matang mengoperasikan Redis HA di Kubernetes,
gunakan managed Redis untuk production critical workload.
```

---

## 8. App Readiness/Liveness with Redis Dependency

Untuk aplikasi Jersey/JAX-RS:

### Liveness

```text id="ops-13"
Liveness should answer:
  is this app process alive?
```

Biasanya **jangan** membuat liveness bergantung pada Redis, karena Redis outage bisa membuat semua pod di-restart terus.

### Readiness

```text id="ops-14"
Readiness should answer:
  can this pod serve traffic correctly?
```

Readiness boleh mempertimbangkan Redis jika Redis critical untuk request path.

| Redis role | Readiness dependency? |
|---|---|
| Optional cache | Tidak perlu down |
| Critical session store | Ya |
| Authz fail-closed cache without fallback | Ya |
| Rate limiter security endpoint | Mungkin |
| Job worker only | HTTP readiness terpisah |
| Pub/Sub notification only | Degraded, tidak perlu full down |
| Idempotency critical command | Mungkin route-specific health lebih baik |

---

## 9. Health Endpoint Design

```json id="ops-15"
{
  "status": "DEGRADED",
  "components": {
    "redis-cache": {
      "status": "DOWN",
      "critical": false,
      "latencyMs": 12
    },
    "redis-session": {
      "status": "UP",
      "critical": true,
      "latencyMs": 3
    },
    "postgresql": {
      "status": "UP",
      "critical": true
    }
  }
}
```

Separate:

```text id="ops-16"
/live
  process health only

/ready
  dependency readiness for traffic

/health/dependencies
  detailed operator view
```

---

## 10. Kubernetes Shutdown

For app pods using Redis:

```text id="ops-17"
1. readiness goes false
2. stop accepting new traffic
3. stop reading Redis Streams / PubSub / queue
4. finish in-flight work within grace period
5. do not ack unfinished jobs
6. release locks if safe
7. close Redis connections
```

For stream workers:

```text id="ops-18"
If job not completed:
  do not XACK
  let pending recovery claim later
```

For locks:

```text id="ops-19"
Release only with token-safe Lua.
If release fails, let TTL expire.
```

---

# Bagian C - HA: Replication, Sentinel, Cluster, Managed Redis

## 11. Redis Replication

Replication gives primary-replica topology.

```text id="ops-20"
primary receives writes
  ↓
replicas copy data
  ↓
replica can be promoted during failover
```

Replication improves availability/read scaling but does not automatically solve every data-loss scenario. Depending on configuration and timing, acknowledged writes may not have reached replica before failure.

For stronger write propagation needs, Redis has commands/features like `WAIT`, but application-level correctness still needs idempotency and durable source-of-truth design.

---

## 12. Redis Sentinel

Redis Sentinel provides high availability for Redis when not using Redis Cluster. It monitors Redis instances, provides notifications, performs automatic failover, and acts as a configuration provider for clients. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/sentinel/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Sentinel is suitable for:

```text id="ops-21"
- primary-replica Redis
- automatic failover
- no sharding requirement
- clients that support Sentinel discovery
```

Sentinel responsibilities:

```text id="ops-22"
- monitor primary/replica
- detect primary failure
- elect/promote replica
- notify clients
- provide current primary address
```

Caveat:

```text id="ops-23"
Sentinel gives HA, not distributed sharding.
Clients must be Sentinel-aware.
Failover still causes transient errors.
```

---

## 13. Redis Cluster

Redis Cluster provides automatic sharding across multiple Redis nodes and some availability during partitions. It can continue when some nodes fail, but becomes unavailable in larger failures such as when the majority of masters are unavailable. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/scaling/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Cluster concepts:

```text id="ops-24"
- 16384 hash slots
- keys mapped to slots
- slots assigned to masters
- replicas for HA
- MOVED/ASK redirects
- hash tags for multi-key co-location
```

Redis Cluster hash tags allow related keys to be allocated to the same hash slot, which is necessary for multi-key operations that must run together. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/reference/cluster-spec/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 14. Cluster Design Implications for Java App

If using Redis Cluster:

```text id="ops-25"
- Java client must be cluster-aware
- multi-key Lua/transaction requires same hash slot
- avoid broad hash tags that create hot slots
- handle MOVED/ASK redirects
- test failover under load
- design keys intentionally
```

Bad multi-key script keys:

```text id="ops-26"
case-svc:prod:tenant:t1:lock:case:c991
case-svc:prod:tenant:t1:fence:case:c991
```

Better with hash tag:

```text id="ops-27"
case-svc:prod:{tenant:t1:case:c991}:lock:submit:v1
case-svc:prod:{tenant:t1:case:c991}:fence:submit:v1
```

---

## 15. Sentinel vs Cluster vs Managed Redis

| Need | Recommended |
|---|---|
| Simple HA, no sharding | Sentinel or managed primary/replica |
| Horizontal scale/sharding | Redis Cluster |
| Low ops burden | Managed Redis |
| Strict custom on-prem control | Self-managed Sentinel/Cluster |
| Multi-key atomic patterns | Non-cluster or careful hash tags |
| Cache-only workload | Managed/serverless cache often enough |
| Queue/security/session critical | Managed HA with backups/security controls |

Practical recommendation:

```text id="ops-28"
For most enterprise microservices:
  use managed Redis when available.

For on-prem:
  prefer documented Sentinel/Cluster deployment,
  automated failover tests,
  and clear runbooks.
```

---

# Bagian D - Persistence, Backup, Restore

## 16. Persistence Mode Selection

Redis persistence options:

| Mode | Meaning | Good for |
|---|---|---|
| No persistence | Data lost on restart | Pure cache |
| RDB | Point-in-time snapshots | Cache warm restart, backup snapshot |
| AOF | Append write operations for replay | More durable Redis state |
| RDB + AOF | Combined trade-off | Some self-managed production setups |

Redis docs define RDB as point-in-time snapshots and AOF as logging every write operation for replay at startup. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/persistence/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 17. Persistence by Workload

| Workload | Suggested persistence |
|---|---|
| DTO cache | None/RDB optional |
| Rate limiter | None usually |
| Lock/semaphore | None usually; TTL/ephemeral |
| Session | AOF/managed HA if Redis primary store |
| Token denylist | AOF/managed HA or fallback auth service |
| Redis Streams queue | AOF/managed HA + DB job backing |
| Idempotency | AOF optional; PostgreSQL final guard required |
| Pub/Sub | N/A |
| Security audit | Do not use Redis primary |

Rule:

```text id="ops-29"
If losing Redis data causes business corruption,
the design is wrong unless another durable source exists.
```

---

## 18. Backup/Restore

For Redis with important state:

```text id="ops-30"
- schedule backup
- test restore
- encrypt backup
- restrict backup access
- document RPO/RTO
- avoid restoring prod data to non-prod without sanitization
```

Restore test should answer:

```text id="ops-31"
- Can Redis start from backup?
- Does app reconnect?
- Are scripts reloaded?
- Are streams/groups intact?
- Are TTLs acceptable?
- Are sessions/token states handled correctly?
```

---

## 19. Disaster Recovery Reality

Redis is often not your final DR source.

```text id="ops-32"
PostgreSQL:
  business recovery

Kafka:
  event replay

Camunda:
  workflow recovery

Redis:
  cache/control-plane/session/queue helper
```

If Redis is lost:

```text id="ops-33"
Cache:
  rebuild

Session:
  forced logout / session recovery policy

Queue:
  DB/outbox re-enqueue

Idempotency:
  PostgreSQL idempotency table

Authz cache:
  reload from source

Notification:
  fetch durable notifications from DB
```

---

# Bagian E - Memory, Eviction, Data Modeling

## 20. Memory Planning

Monitor:

```text id="ops-34"
used_memory
used_memory_rss
mem_fragmentation_ratio
maxmemory
evicted_keys
expired_keys
keyspace_hits
keyspace_misses
big keys
hot keys
```

Capacity planning must include:

```text id="ops-35"
- average key/value size
- cardinality by key family
- TTL
- peak traffic
- stream retention
- fragmentation
- replication overhead
- persistence fork/copy-on-write overhead
```

---

## 21. Eviction Policy Selection

Eviction must match workload.

| Workload | Eviction |
|---|---|
| Cache-only | `allkeys-lru`, `allkeys-lfu`, or workload-specific |
| TTL cache | `volatile-lru`/TTL-aware if only TTL keys should evict |
| Session/security | Avoid eviction; separate/noeviction |
| Queue/stream | Avoid eviction |
| Lock/semaphore | Avoid eviction or accept coordination risk |
| Mixed workload | Avoid if possible; split instances |

Rule:

```text id="ops-36"
If not all keys are equally disposable,
do not put them in same evicting Redis instance.
```

---

## 22. Big Key / Hot Key

Big key examples:

```text id="ops-37"
- one Hash with millions of fields
- one Set per tenant with millions of members
- one Stream never trimmed
- one String with huge JSON blob
```

Hot key examples:

```text id="ops-38"
- global rate limit counter
- global config key hit by every request
- one tenant key for all users
- one Pub/Sub channel with extreme fanout
```

Mitigation:

```text id="ops-39"
- shard by tenant/user/route
- cap collection size
- trim streams
- split data structure
- use local cache carefully
- avoid huge JSON blobs
- monitor hot keys if tooling supports
```

---

## 23. Stream Retention

Redis Streams can grow indefinitely if not trimmed.

Pattern:

```text id="ops-40"
XADD stream MAXLEN ~ 100000 * ...
XTRIM stream MAXLEN ~ 100000
```

But:

```text id="ops-41"
Do not trim entries still needed for pending recovery.
```

Recommended:

```text id="ops-42"
Important job state:
  PostgreSQL table

Redis Stream:
  execution queue with bounded retention
```

---

# Bagian F - Monitoring & Observability

## 24. Redis INFO

Use Redis `INFO` for high-level metrics:

```text id="ops-43"
server
clients
memory
persistence
stats
replication
cpu
cluster
keyspace
commandstats
```

Redis’s observability guidance highlights using `INFO`, `SLOWLOG`, latency monitoring, and external tools such as Prometheus/Grafana or APM platforms to monitor memory, latency, throughput, and connected clients. ([Redis](https://redis.io/tutorials/operate/redis-at-scale/observability/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

---

## 25. Slowlog

Redis Slow Log records queries exceeding a configured execution time threshold. The recorded execution time excludes client I/O and includes the time Redis spends executing the command while the server thread is blocked. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/commands/slowlog/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Use slowlog to detect:

```text id="ops-44"
- slow Lua scripts
- heavy commands
- unexpected KEYS/SCAN misuse
- large sorted set/hash operations
- stream operations under load
```

Operational commands:

```text id="ops-45"
SLOWLOG GET 128
SLOWLOG LEN
SLOWLOG RESET
```

Do not expose slowlog with sensitive command arguments to broad audiences.

---

## 26. Latency Monitoring

Redis latency troubleshooting docs define latency as delay between issuing a command and receiving reply, and note that Redis processing is usually very low but certain conditions can cause higher latency. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/optimization/latency/?utm_source=chatgpt.com)) Redis also has a latency monitoring framework for detecting latency spikes. ([Redis](https://redis.io/docs/latest/operate/oss_and_stack/management/optimization/latency-monitor/?utm_source=chatgpt.com))

Monitor:

```text id="ops-46"
- command latency p50/p95/p99
- Redis server-side latency
- client-side timeout
- network latency
- failover reconnect time
- Lua script latency
```

Latency spike causes:

```text id="ops-47"
- big keys
- slow commands
- persistence fork/copy-on-write
- CPU saturation
- memory pressure/swap
- network issue
- noisy neighbor
- huge pipeline batch
- client connection storm
```

---

## 27. Application Metrics

For Java/Jersey app:

```text id="ops-48"
redis.command.count
redis.command.latency_ms
redis.command.timeout.count
redis.command.error.count
redis.connection.active
redis.connection.reconnect.count
redis.pool.active
redis.pool.wait_ms
redis.lua.latency_ms
redis.lua.error.count
redis.cache.hit.count
redis.cache.miss.count
redis.stream.pending.count
redis.stream.dlq.depth
redis.pubsub.reconnect.count
```

Labels:

```text id="ops-49"
service
env
redis_role
operation_family
command_family
result
exception_class
```

Avoid:

```text id="ops-50"
raw key
tenant ID raw
user ID
token
idempotency key
full exception with secret URI
```

---

## 28. Dashboard Sections

Recommended dashboard:

```text id="ops-51"
1. Availability
   - Redis up/down
   - connected clients
   - reconnects
   - failovers

2. Latency
   - command p95/p99
   - slowlog count
   - timeout count

3. Memory
   - used memory
   - fragmentation
   - evicted keys
   - expired keys

4. Persistence
   - last save status
   - AOF status
   - fork time
   - backup status

5. Replication/HA
   - primary/replica link
   - replication lag
   - role changes

6. Workload-specific
   - cache hit ratio
   - rate limit decisions
   - stream lag/pending/DLQ
   - session/token errors
   - lock contention
```

# Bagian G - Java Client Timeout, Retry, Circuit Breaker

## 29. Timeout Budget

Redis calls in request path must be short.

Example:

```text id="ops-52"
HTTP request timeout:
  2 seconds

Service business budget:
  1.5 seconds

Redis cache get:
  20-100 ms

Redis rate limit:
  20-100 ms

Redis idempotency:
  50-200 ms depending criticality

Redis session validation:
  50-200 ms
```

Rule:

```text id="ops-53"
Redis timeout must be shorter than HTTP timeout.
Redis slowness must not exhaust Jersey request threads.
```

---

## 30. Retry Policy

Not every Redis command should be retried.

| Operation | Retry? | Notes |
|---|---:|---|
| Cache GET | Maybe 0-1 | Fallback DB |
| Cache SET | Usually no | Best effort |
| Rate limit check | Usually no aggressive retry | Fail policy |
| Idempotency check | Careful retry same key | Avoid duplicate |
| Lock acquire | Short retry/jitter optional | Avoid long wait |
| Safe unlock | No repeated storm; TTL fallback | Log |
| Stream XACK | Retry/check pending | Important |
| Pub/Sub publish | If durable notification stored, outbox retry | Pub/Sub itself no guarantee |
| Session validation | Maybe fallback/fail-closed | Security-sensitive |

Rule:

```text id="ops-54"
Retries must be bounded, jittered, and operation-aware.
```

---

## 31. Fail-Open vs Fail-Closed

| Redis use | Failure policy |
|---|---|
| Optional cache | Fail-open / fallback DB |
| Auth session store | Fail-closed |
| JWT denylist high-risk | Fail-closed or fallback auth source |
| Authz cache | Fallback source; fail-closed if unavailable |
| Rate limiter public read | Possibly fail-open |
| Rate limiter login/admin | Fail-closed/security-biased |
| Idempotency command | Prefer fail-closed or DB fallback |
| Lock for cache rebuild | Skip work |
| Semaphore for external API | Fail-closed/defer |
| Pub/Sub notification | Degrade realtime; durable DB remains |

---

## 32. Circuit Breaker

Use circuit breaker when Redis is unstable.

```text id="ops-55"
Redis timeouts spike
  ↓
open circuit for optional cache
  ↓
skip Redis and use DB/fallback
  ↓
periodically half-open
```

But for critical Redis:

```text id="ops-56"
session store down:
  return 503/401 depending contract
```

Circuit breaker must be per Redis role:

```text id="ops-57"
redis-cache circuit
redis-session circuit
redis-stream circuit
redis-rate-limit circuit
```

Do not use one global circuit for all Redis use cases unless roles share same failure semantics.

---

# Bagian H - Failover Behavior

## 33. What App Sees During Failover

During Sentinel/Cluster/managed failover, Java app may see:

```text id="ops-58"
- connection reset
- timeout
- READONLY error
- MOVED/ASK redirect
- temporary command failure
- duplicate connection attempts
- Pub/Sub disconnect/reconnect
- pending stream reads interrupted
```

App must handle:

```text id="ops-59"
- reconnect
- retry safe operations
- reload Lua scripts if NOSCRIPT
- re-subscribe Pub/Sub
- recreate/read consumer group if needed
- resume stream processing
```

---

## 34. Data Consistency During Failover

Redis failover may lose recent writes depending replication/persistence timing. For example:

```text id="ops-60"
write accepted by primary
primary fails before replica receives write
replica promoted
write lost
```

Therefore:

```text id="ops-61"
Redis should not be the only final store for critical business events.
```

Use:

```text id="ops-62"
PostgreSQL unique constraints
idempotency table
outbox
job table
reconciliation worker
provider idempotency key
```

---

## 35. Pub/Sub During Failover

Pub/Sub subscribers may disconnect and miss messages.

Design:

```text id="ops-63"
Pub/Sub signal lost
  ↓
client/pod reconnects
  ↓
resync from PostgreSQL/projection/API
```

For notification:

```text id="ops-64"
notification row exists
Pub/Sub only wakes online clients
```

---

## 36. Streams During Failover

For Redis Streams:

```text id="ops-65"
worker XREADGROUP interrupted
  ↓
reconnect
  ↓
continue read
  ↓
recover pending via XPENDING/XAUTOCLAIM
```

But important jobs should also have PostgreSQL job state so worker can reconcile if Redis stream state is uncertain.

---

## 37. Lua Scripts After Failover

After reconnect/failover:

```text id="ops-66"
EVALSHA may return NOSCRIPT
```

Client must:

```text id="ops-67"
catch NOSCRIPT
SCRIPT LOAD
retry EVALSHA
```

This is especially important if scripts are loaded in memory of old primary and new primary/script cache differs.

---

# Bagian I - Backup & Operational Runbooks

## 38. Runbook: Redis Latency Spike

Check:

```text id="ops-68"
1. Is Redis CPU high?
2. Memory pressure or swap?
3. SLOWLOG entries?
4. Big key operation?
5. Persistence fork/AOF rewrite?
6. Network latency?
7. Client connection storm?
8. Pipeline batch too large?
9. Hot key?
10. Recent deploy introduced Lua/script?
```

Immediate mitigations:

```text id="ops-69"
- disable non-critical cache traffic via circuit breaker
- reduce worker concurrency
- pause bulk cleanup/warmup
- lower pipeline batch size
- route traffic if HA topology supports
- scale/readjust client pools carefully
```

---

## 39. Runbook: Memory Near Max

Check:

```text id="ops-70"
1. top key families by cardinality
2. streams not trimmed
3. missing TTL keys
4. big hashes/sets/zsets
5. cache TTL too long
6. unexpected tenant spike
7. memory fragmentation
8. eviction rate
```

Immediate mitigations:

```text id="ops-71"
- reduce TTL for cache families
- trim streams
- delete known safe cache namespace via SCAN+UNLINK
- stop cache warmup
- scale memory/cluster
- split workload
```

Do not:

```text id="ops-72"
FLUSHDB on shared production Redis
```

---

## 40. Runbook: Redis Down

Classify Redis role:

```text id="ops-73"
cache Redis down:
  fallback DB
  open cache circuit
  expect DB load increase

session Redis down:
  auth endpoints fail/degrade
  communicate outage
  avoid restart storm

queue Redis down:
  pause workers
  rely on DB/outbox
  resume and reconcile

coordination Redis down:
  disable leader/scheduler or use fallback
  avoid duplicate work

pubsub Redis down:
  realtime degraded
  clients poll/fetch
```

---

## 41. Runbook: Failover Test

Regularly test:

```text id="ops-74"
- Sentinel failover
- Cluster primary failover
- managed Redis failover event
- app reconnect
- Lua NOSCRIPT reload
- Pub/Sub resubscribe
- Stream pending recovery
- session behavior
- idempotency correctness
- latency during failover
```

Success criteria:

```text id="ops-75"
- no business data corruption
- no duplicate external side effects
- no permanent worker stuck
- recovery within RTO
- alerts fire
- dashboards show issue
```

---

## 42. Runbook: Bad Deployment Introduced Redis Bug

Examples:

```text id="ops-76"
- keys missing TTL
- huge key cardinality
- slow Lua script
- wrong key prefix deletes wrong tenant
- pipeline batch too large
```

Mitigation:

```text id="ops-77"
1. rollback app
2. disable feature flag
3. inspect key family
4. dry-run cleanup
5. SCAN+UNLINK safe namespace
6. add regression test
7. update key registry
```

---

# Bagian J - Production Checklist

## 43. Redis Key Family Registry

Maintain a registry:

```yaml id="ops-78"
- family: case-summary-cache
  service: case-svc
  redis_role: cache
  key_pattern: case-svc:{env}:tenant:{tenantHash}:cache:case-summary:v1:case:{caseHash}
  ttl: 300s
  source_of_truth: postgresql.cases
  contains_pii: false
  eviction_allowed: true
  fail_policy: fallback_db
  owner: case-platform-team

- family: session
  service: auth-svc
  redis_role: security
  key_pattern: auth-svc:{env}:tenant:{tenantHash}:session:v1:sid:{sessionHash}
  ttl: idle 30m + absolute 8h
  source_of_truth: redis/auth-db
  contains_pii: minimal
  eviction_allowed: false
  fail_policy: fail_closed
  owner: identity-team
```

---

## 44. Deployment Checklist

```text id="ops-79"
[ ] Redis role/workload classified
[ ] Persistence mode selected
[ ] Eviction policy selected
[ ] Memory max set and monitored
[ ] TLS/network/ACL configured
[ ] Backup/restore tested if needed
[ ] Sentinel/Cluster/managed HA tested
[ ] Java client supports topology
[ ] Timeouts configured per Redis role
[ ] Retry policy operation-aware
[ ] Pub/Sub reconnect/resubscribe tested
[ ] Stream pending recovery tested
[ ] Lua NOSCRIPT reload implemented
[ ] Metrics/dashboard/alerts available
[ ] Runbooks available
```

---

## 45. Java Client Checklist

```text id="ops-80"
[ ] Single client lifecycle managed at app startup/shutdown
[ ] Separate connections for blocking/PubSub/normal commands
[ ] Command timeout short and explicit
[ ] Connection timeout explicit
[ ] Pooling/multiplexing understood
[ ] Sentinel/Cluster mode configured correctly
[ ] TLS/ACL credentials loaded securely
[ ] Redis errors mapped by use case
[ ] Circuit breaker per Redis role
[ ] Script registry handles NOSCRIPT
[ ] Metrics around latency/errors/timeouts
[ ] No raw key/token in logs
```

---

## 46. Alert Examples

```text id="ops-81"
Redis unavailable > 1 minute
Redis command timeout rate > threshold
Redis p99 latency > SLO
Redis memory > 85%
Evicted keys > 0 for security/queue Redis
Stream pending oldest age > threshold
DLQ depth > 0 for critical jobs
Replication link down
AOF/RDB persistence error
Pub/Sub subscriber disconnected
Lua script error spike
Cache hit ratio collapse
Session validation Redis errors
```

For cache Redis, eviction may be normal. For security/session/queue Redis, eviction should usually page or at least alert strongly.

---

# Bagian K - Common Anti-Patterns

## 47. Anti-Patterns

| Anti-pattern | Dampak | Solusi |
|---|---|---|
| One Redis for everything | Blast radius besar | Split by workload/role |
| Cache eviction policy on session/queue keys | Data/security issue | Separate/noeviction |
| Liveness depends on Redis | Restart storm | Redis in readiness only if critical |
| No command timeout | Thread exhaustion | Explicit short timeout |
| Aggressive retry on request path | Amplifies outage | Bounded retry/circuit breaker |
| Pub/Sub as durable notification | Message loss | DB notification + Pub/Sub signal |
| Redis-only critical queue | Job loss on failover/data loss | DB job/outbox backing |
| No stream trimming | Memory growth | Retention policy |
| Lua script not monitored | Hidden latency | script metrics/slowlog |
| Cluster used without key hash planning | CROSSSLOT errors | hash tags/key design |
| No failover test | Surprises during incident | scheduled chaos/failover test |
| No backup restore test | False DR confidence | restore drill |
| Raw Redis URI in logs | Secret leak | redact |
| `KEYS *` production | Redis stall | SCAN/runbook |

---

## 48. Final Senior Engineer Checklist

```text id="ops-82"
[ ] Apa Redis role utama: cache/security/queue/coordination/pubsub?
[ ] Apa yang terjadi jika Redis hilang total?
[ ] Apakah ada source of truth durable?
[ ] Apakah persistence mode sesuai workload?
[ ] Apakah eviction policy sesuai workload?
[ ] Apakah HA topology dipilih: managed/Sentinel/Cluster?
[ ] Apakah Java client topology-aware?
[ ] Apakah failover sudah diuji?
[ ] Apakah timeout/retry/circuit breaker per use case?
[ ] Apakah liveness/readiness benar?
[ ] Apakah stream/job/pending/DLQ bisa direcover?
[ ] Apakah Pub/Sub loss bisa ditoleransi?
[ ] Apakah security/session keys tidak bisa dievict?
[ ] Apakah dashboards dan alerts ada?
[ ] Apakah runbook Redis down/latency/memory/failover tersedia?
```

---

## 49. Ringkasan Praktis

```text id="ops-83"
Docker:
  untuk local dev/test, bukan HA production

Kubernetes:
  hati-hati dengan stateful Redis; managed Redis sering lebih aman

Sentinel:
  HA untuk non-cluster Redis

Redis Cluster:
  sharding + HA, tapi butuh key slot/hash tag discipline

Persistence:
  RDB snapshot, AOF replay log, atau no persistence sesuai workload

Monitoring:
  INFO, SLOWLOG, latency, memory, replication, stream lag, app metrics

Failover:
  expect transient errors, reconnect, NOSCRIPT, Pub/Sub reconnect, stream recovery

Java client:
  explicit timeout, retry by operation, circuit breaker by Redis role

Production rule:
  Redis boleh hilang tanpa business corruption karena PostgreSQL/Kafka/Camunda/outbox tetap menjaga truth
```

Kalimat kunci:

> Redis production yang sehat adalah Redis yang perannya jelas. Cache boleh hilang dan dibangun ulang. Session/security harus fail-safe. Queue harus punya recovery. Pub/Sub boleh hilang karena hanya signal. Semua business truth harus tetap bisa direkonstruksi dari PostgreSQL, Kafka, Camunda, atau audit store.

```text id="ops-84"
7. Cache Invalidation, Stampede Protection & Negative Cache
8. Keyspace Notification Pattern
9. Rate Limiting in JAX-RS Filter
10. Idempotency Key, Request Deduplication & Replay Protection
11. Redis-backed Job Queue
12. Redis Streams Consumer Group Pattern
13. Pub/Sub vs Keyspace Notification vs Streams vs Kafka Boundary
14. Distributed Coordination Pattern
15. Distributed Lock, Redlock, Lease & Fencing Token
16. Distributed Concurrency Control

20. Realtime Notification & WebSocket Support
21. Security, Multi-Tenant Isolation & Compliance
22. Production Operations: Docker, Kubernetes, HA, Monitoring, Failover
```

Rekomendasi materi berikutnya: **Java Elasticsearch / OpenSearch in Jersey/JAX-RS microservice application**, terutama untuk search, indexing pipeline, outbox-based indexing, query DSL, pagination, relevance, zero-downtime reindexing, dan boundary dengan PostgreSQL sebagai source of truth.
